#### Lesson 1 — SCHOOL-RELATED WORDS (p.88–92)

- **Skills blend** : SPEAKING INTO READING (bandeau p.88)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading — Exploiting an ad
- **Communicating** : Speaking — Expanding vocabulary
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Persuasion ; Word building
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.88) : nuage de 15 bulles-questions sur le lexique scolaire + un encadré de
18 items lexicaux. (p.89) Encadré-amorce « The real wealth of a nation is … » ; photo d'une
fillette mangeant à la cuillère. (p.89–90) **Publicité du World Food Programme (United Nations)**,
titre imprimé « **The real wealth of Asia** », sous-titre « **They are the future** » ; corps de
texte ≈ 120 mots : l'éducation est une priorité pour l'Asie, des millions d'enfants en sont privés
faute de moyens, le WFP fournit depuis 40 ans des repas nutritifs à l'école, ce qui réduit la faim
et augmente la fréquentation ; appel au don. Coordonnées imprimées : www.wfp.org et
asia.fundraising@wfp.org.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.88> **IN PAIRS** — activité **1** : « Here is a list of words and phrases for you to match
   with the corresponding questions. **Student A: Ask the questions. Student B: Answer them.** »
   Encadré lexical imprimé : *geometry, art, physics, chemistry, languages, on the timetable,
   terms/semesters, primary school, in a dictionary, to multiply, to subtract, to divide,
   paragraph, chapters, full stop, Ordinary and Advanced, sciences, margin*.
   Les 15 bulles-questions imprimées : « What is a part of a text called ? » • « What school do you
   attend when you are 8 ? » • « What are books divided into? » • « What does [ / ] mean? » • « What
   do the « O » and « A » stand for in O level and A level? » • « What does [ - ] mean? » • « Where
   can you find the days of the week? » • « What do you call the space on the left side of the
   page? » • « Where can you find the meaning of words? » • « What do you find at the end of a
   sentence? » • « What subjects do you study at secondary schools? » • « How is the school year
   divided ? » • « What do you call English, French and German together? » • « What are physics,
   chemistry and biology together called? » • « What does [x] mean? »
2. <p.89> Activité **2 — THE OPPOSITES GAME** : a) « Play the opposites game by providing a word
   for which your partner will suggest an opposite. Use the words in the box. » Encadré :
   *teach – clever – good – hard – pass – written – first – practical – oral – candidate – stupid –
   fail – worse – attend – examiner – bad – easy – last – learn – work – theoretical – leave –
   holiday – better –*. b) « Think of more opposites related to education and check with classmates
   to get more words. »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « teach-learn ; clever-stupid ; good-bad ; hard-easy ; pass-fail ;
   practical-theoretical ; candidate-examiner ; worse-better ; attend-leave ; last-first ;
   work-holiday. » (le couple *written / oral*, présent dans l'encadré, n'est pas repris par le guide)
3. <p.89> **READING** — activité **1** : « Focus on this incomplete sentence and jot down as many
   alternatives as you can to complete it » → « The real wealth of a nation is ... »
4. <p.89> **READING** — activité **2** : « Look at the title and the subtitle of the ad and guess
   a) What the real wealth of a nation is. b) What "they" refers to. c) What the ad is about. »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 2.b) they refers to the children. »
5. <p.89> **READING** — activité **3** : « Read the ad and answer the questions below.
   a) What does 'WFP' stand for? What is its role? Focus on the website and e-mail address to find
   out. b) What is the real wealth of Asia? »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 3. WFP = World Food Programme ».
6. <p.90> Activité **4** : « What makes children stay in school? »
7. <p.90> Activité **5** : « What do you think the purpose of this ad is? a) Showing the importance
   of education. b) Persuading people to donate money. c) Describing poor children's situation. »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 5. the correct answer is b ».
8. <p.90> Activité **6** : « Fill in the diagram with the 3 consequences of. » [sic — la consigne
   s'arrête là]. Diagramme imprimé : boîte-mère « **Providing nutritious meals at school** » reliée
   à 3 boîtes vides.
9. <p.90> Activité **7** : « Find words that mean : a) offering, giving ; b) a very important
   thing ; c) refused ; d) nourishing, healthy ; e) makes better. »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « a = providing ; b = priority ; c = denied ; d = nutritious ; e = boosts. »
10. <p.91> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : « Look at this sentence from the ad. *Their parents
    are **too** poor **to** send them to school.* What do you notice? What does this express? »
    Règle à compléter (encadré) : `too + ? + ? ⇨ ?`. Puis « Use 'too ….. to' to combine the
    sentences » : a) *You are very busy. You can't finish all the things you planned to do.*
    b) *I'm very tired. I can't help you.* c) *It's late. We can't go to the cinema.*
    *Clé (guide p.24)* : « a) You are too busy to finish what you planned to do. b) I am too tired
    to help you. c) It is too late to go to the cinema. »
11. <p.91> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **2** : « Complete the spidergrams with the corresponding
    word form. » Trois spidergrammes imprimés : **Noun** (amorces *denial*, *provision*),
    **Verb** (amorces *attend*, *reduce*), **Adjective** (amorce *expectant*).
    *Clé (guide p.24)* : pas de corrigé, consigne à l'enseignant — « Get the students to write words
    related to the lesson in the various forms suggested in the spidergrams. Thisn [sic] is an
    opportunity to revise vocabulary and word forms. »
12. <p.91> Encadré **AN ACROSTIC ABOUT SCHOOL** : *Study / Communicate / Help / Offer / Organise /
    Learn*. « Can you write your own acrostic about school? » → grille à compléter
    `S…… C…… H…… O…… O…… L……`

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.92

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> boost, deny, nutritious, priority, provide

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> Too + adjective + to
> They are too poor to send their children to school : they are extremely poor
> so it is impossible for them to send their children to school.

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Complete the title of an ad
> • Predict what an ad is about
> • Identify synonyms in context
> • What else have you learnt in this lesson?

**Lexique de la leçon** : *boost, deny, nutritious, priority, provide* (MY NEW WORDS) ; lexique
scolaire de l'activité 1 (*geometry, art, physics, chemistry, languages, on the timetable,
terms/semesters, primary school, in a dictionary, to multiply, to subtract, to divide, paragraph,
chapters, full stop, Ordinary and Advanced, sciences, margin*) ; couples d'opposés de l'activité 2 ;
formes dérivées *denial / provision / attend / reduce / expectant*.

#### Lesson 2 — EDUCATION FOR ALL (p.93–96)

- **Skills blend** : SPEAKING INTO READING (bandeau p.93)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading — Exploiting a graph and data ; Reading an ad
- **Communicating** : Speaking — Understanding acronyms
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Expressing opinion ; Hypothesizing ; **If / Unless**
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.93) : photo de classe surpeuplée ; tableau à 2 colonnes *Acronym / Organization*
(11 lignes) ; **camembert « REGIONAL DISTRIBUTION OF CHILDREN OUT OF SCHOOL, 2001/02 »** (parts
imprimées 36.7 %, 20.8 %, 18.6 %, 8.7 %, 7.6 %, 2.8 %, 2.5 %, 2.3 % ; légende : Central and Eastern
Europe, Middle East and North Africa, Eastern and Southern Africa, West and Central Africa, South
Asia, East Asia and the Pacific, Latin America and Caribbean, Industrialized countries ; crédit
graphique « Friedrich Huebler, February 2006, huebler.blogspot.com » ; source imprimée « Data
source: UNESCO Institute for Statistics (UIS). 2005. *Children out of school: Measuring exclusion
from primary education*. Montreal: UIS. Table 1.1, page 18. »). (p.94) Extrait UIS ≈ 110 mots
détaillant la répartition régionale des 115 millions d'enfants non scolarisés. (p.95) **Publicité
UNICEF** à mots tronqués, visuel « Grade 2 + $1 = Grade 3 », slogan « **SAVE A CHILD AND THE FUTURE
WILL FOLLOW** », lien www.unicef.org. (p.95) Citation d'Aristote.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.93> **IN PAIRS** — activité **1** : « Match the acronyms with the corresponding organisations.
   Write each acronym where appropriate. » Acronymes imprimés : *NOW - UNO - WWF - WTO - WHO -
   UNESCO - FAO - WFP - UNICEF - IMF - OPEC*. Organisations : a) World Food Programme ;
   b) International Monetary Fund ; c) United Nations International Children's Emergency Fund ;
   d) Food and Agriculture Organization ; e) United Nations Educational Scientific and Cultural
   Organization ; f) World Wide Fund ; g) World Trade Organization ; h) Oil Producing and Exporting
   Countries ; i) United Nations Organization ; j) National Organization of Women ; k) World Health
   Organization.
   *Clé* : non donnée par le guide (l'appariement est mécanique — chaque acronyme est l'initiale de
   l'organisation en regard).
2. <p.93> Activité **2** : « Which organizations can bring solutions for children out of school?
   Discuss with a partner. »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 2- UNICEF – UNESCO ».
   *Note (guide p.24, « Useful hints »)* : « UNICEF : The United Nations International Children's
   Emergency Fund was created in 1946. Its purpose is to uphold children's rights and provide them
   with food, medicines, health care, education and a decent living. » — « UNESCO : The United
   Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization was founded in 1945. Itd [sic] role is
   to contribute to peace and security by promoting collaboration among nations through educational,
   scientific and cultural relations of the people of the world. » — « WFP : The World Food
   Programme was created in 1963 to fight against hunger in the world. It is based in Rome, Italy. »
3. <p.94> Activité **1** : « Study the graph and check which countries suffer most from exclusion.
   Try to understand why. »
4. <p.94> Activité **2** : « Read the UIS extract to complete the table with the appropriate
   figures. » Tableau imprimé *Region / Percentage / Number of children*, cases partiellement
   remplies : West and Central Africa **20.8 %** / ---- ; Eastern and Southern Africa ---- /
   **21.4 million** ; East Asia and the Pacific **8.7 %** / ---- ; Middle East and North Africa
   ---- / ---- ; Latin America and The Caribbean ---- / **3.3 million** ; Central and Eastern Europe
   ---- / ---- ; Industrialized Countries **2.3 %** / ----.
   *Clé* : non redonnée par le guide ; les valeurs manquantes sont **lisibles telles quelles dans
   l'extrait UIS imprimé** — West and Central Africa 24.0 million ; Eastern and Southern Africa
   18.6 % ; East Asia and the Pacific 10.0 million ; Middle East and North Africa 7.6 % /
   8.8 million ; Latin America and the Caribbean 2.8 % ; Central and Eastern Europe 2.5 % /
   2.9 million ; Industrialized Countries 2.6 million. (South Asia — 42.3 million, 36.7 % — figure
   dans l'extrait mais pas dans le tableau.)
5. <p.94> Activité **3** : « Find the words that mean : a) registration ; b) consequently. »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 3- a) enrollment [sic — le manuel écrit *enrolment* dans MY NEW WORDS] –
   b) therefore ».
6. <p.94–95> **A UNICEF AD** — activité **1** : « Some of the words in the following ad are
   unfinished. Complete them. The first letter(s) of each word is provided. Each dot stands for a
   letter. » Texte à trous imprimé : « Give to UNICEF and you give children a *ch….* For example
   *j…* $1 can *p……* 8 children with pencils and exercise *b….* . Or $225 can buy a recreation kit
   *con…….* toys, *g….* and physical education supplies for an entire *c….* of girls and boys. To
   learn more, or to give, *p…..* visit www.unicef.org »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 1- chance - just - provide - books - containing - games - class - please. »
7. <p.95> Activité **2** : « What is the purpose of this ad? »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 2- persuade people to donate money ».
8. <p.95> Activité **3** : « What is used to draw the reader's attention? »
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 3- an equation ».
9. <p.95> Activité **4** : « Rephrase the UNICEF's equation *Grade 2 + $1 = Grade 3*. Start as
   shown. a) If we don't--- b) Unless --- What is the difference between sentence 'a' and sentence
   'b'? » Puis encadré « Draw a conclusion : `if + ? verb = unless + ? verb` ».
   *Clé (guide p.24)* : « 4-a) If we don't give to UNICEF children won't have a chance.
   b) Unless we give to UNICEF children won't have a chance. A contains a negative verb after IF but
   b an affirmative verb after UNLESS. » + consigne enseignant : « Draw students' attention to this
   difference and do the exercises in the GRAMMAR FOCUS section. »
10. <p.95> Activité **5** : « How can a child be saved? »
11. <p.95> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : « Read this quotation by Aristotle and rephrase it
    in your own words : *The fountain of knowledge will dry up unless it is continuously replenished
    by streams of new learning.* »
12. <p.95–96> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **2** : « Express the sentences below differently
    beginning as shown. » 7 items : a) *Unless we help poor children, they can't have a good future.*
    → If ……… ; b) *If they don't get an education, they will not live a decent life.* → Unless ……… ;
    c) *They will not succeed unless we provide them with the necessary tools.* → If ……… ;
    d) *Their life will not be meaningful if their conditions are not improved.* → Unless ……… ;
    e) *This situation will not change unless everyone contributes with some assistance.* → If ……… ;
    f) *Unless we all feel concerned, this change will not be possible.* → If ……… ;
    g) *If we do not get involved as soon as possible, the situation will get worse.* → Unless ………
    *Clé* : non donnée par le guide.
13. <p.96> **WEBQUEST** : « For more details about international organisations, choose 1 or 2
    acronyms and use the Google or Yahoo search engine to select the most interesting information
    and insert it in your PORTFOLIO. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.96

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> acronyms, enrolment, therefore

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> If + negative verb
>          =
> Unless + positive verb

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Interpret a graph
> • Read an ad
> • Guess the meaning of words

**Lexique de la leçon** : *acronyms, enrolment, therefore* (MY NEW WORDS) ; les 11 acronymes
d'organisations internationales et leurs développés ; lexique du don et de la scolarisation
(*donate, recreation kit, exercise books, out of school, exclusion, primary school enrolment rates*).

#### Lesson 3 — VIRTUAL SCHOOLS (p.97–100)

- **Skills blend** : LISTENING INTO SPEAKING (bandeau p.97)
- **Constructing meaning** : Listening to a report
- **Communicating** : Speaking — role play
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Expressing agreement and disagreement ; **Emphatic form**
- **Pronunciation** : Emphasizing words in a sentence ; Silent letters

**Supports** (p.97) : photo d'un enfant devant un ordinateur portable ; encadré **TIMETABLE** —
`0900 Log on to the internet, chat to classmates in the "school hall"` / `0930 Classes start` /
`1030 Break` / `1100 Lesson 2` / `1200 Lunch` / `1300 Last class` / `1400 "Home time"`.
**Document sonore en 2 parties** (reportage sur les écoles virtuelles ; script non reproduit ici —
il figure dans le tapescript du guide). (p.98) Couverture de l'ouvrage *VIRTUAL SCHOOLS — PLANNING
FOR SUCCESS*, edited by Zane L. Berge and Tom Clark.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.97> Activité **1** : « Look at the picture, the title of the lesson and the timetable to guess
   what the child is doing. »
2. <p.97> Activité **2** : « Listen to Part 1 and complete the sentence below. » — « In ……… 2006,
   Shan Jayran and her ……… John Davies ……… First College, an ……… high school and now it has ………
   students. »
   *Clé (guide p.25)* : « 2/ January – husband – opened – on-line – 22 . »
3. <p.97> Activité **3** : « Match the words on the left with their definitions on the right. There
   is an extra definition. » 1. bullying / 2. log on / 3. appeal / 4. texting / 5. bustle ↔
   a) writing on the computer ; b) crowd of people moving in a busy way ; c) testing ; d) connect a
   computer system ; e) what makes something attractive ; f) frighten or hurt a weaker person.
   *Clé (guide p.25)* : « 3/ 1-f / 2-d / 3-e / 4-a / 5-b » (la définition **c) testing** est
   l'intruse).
4. <p.98> Activité **4** : « Listen to Part 2 and complete the table with the appropriate
   information. » Tableau *Name / Age / Reason for enrolling in virtual schools* — lignes Zack et
   Natalie.
   *Clé (guide p.25)* : Zack, 12 — « Suffering panic attacks and bullying » ; Natalie, 13 — « Afraid
   of speaking in class / strict teacher ».
5. <p.98> Activité **5** : « What is the journalist's opinion about virtual schools? Justify your
   answer. »
6. <p.98> Activité **6** : « What are the benefits and drawbacks of virtual schools? »
7. <p.98> **SPEAKING / IN PAIRS** : « Use the information you gathered from the Listening for a
   conversation about virtual schools. Student A is in favour of virtual schools while student B is
   not. Be ready to enact it. » Amorces imposées : `- I think…` / `- I'm afraid I don't agree…` /
   `- I see what you mean, but…` / `- You are making a good point, however…` / `- What you say is
   ……………. but I still strongly believe…`
8. <p.98> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : « "Sitting at a computer for 5 hours **does** have
   side-effects." Why does the speaker raise the pitch of his voice when he pronounces *does*?
   Complete the rule. » Encadré : `We use : ? + infinitive without to → to ? meaning`.
   *Clé (guide p.25)* : « 1. We use do+verb to emphasize meaning. »
9. <p.98> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **2** : « Complete the paragraph with the words in the box. »
   Encadré : *bustling – enrol – texting – bullying – do - log on - more*. Paragraphe : « The students
   who ------ in virtual schools ------ feel better because they no longer suffer from ------ or
   ------. They are ------ confident: all they have to do is ------ and start ------. »
   *Clé (guide p.25)* : « 2. Complete the paragraph : enrol – do – bullying – bustling – more –
   log on – texting. »
10. <p.99> **PRONUNCIATION FOCUS** — activité **1** : « Read these words aloud and check what they
    have in common, What do you notice? » — *right – half – taught – friend – bustle – psychology –
    knowledge – comb – knee – through.*
    *Clé (guide p.25)* : « 1. All the words contain silent letters ».
11. <p.99> **PRONUNCIATION FOCUS** — activité **2** : « Focus on the underlined letters. Pronounce
    all the letters in bold to get the past form of a semi-regular verb. » — *plum**b**er –
    bis**cu**its – ha**r**mful – autum**n** – lis**t**en.*
    *Clé (guide p.25)* : « 2. The verb is "burnt". »
12. <p.99> **PRONUNCIATION FOCUS** — activité **3** : « Think of a word you will get from silent
    letters. Write the words that contain the silent letters you need. Underline the silent letter in
    each word. Say the words to the class. »
13. <p.99> Encadré **Emphasizing words in a sentence** : phrase support « **I** read a tale last
    week. » — « Say the sentence 5 times stressing one word each time you say it. Then answer the
    questions. Focus on the words in bold type. » Exemple imprimé : *Who read a tale last week?* →
    « **I** read a tale last week. » Puis : *What did you read?* / *What did you do last week?* /
    *When did you read a tale?*
14. <p.100> Prolongement : phrase support « Mary enrolled in a virtual school last summer. » — « Ask
    and answer questions using **who, what, when and where**. Follow the example above. Make sure you
    emphasize the appropriate word corresponding to the question asked. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.100

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> appeal, bullying, bustle, log on, texting

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> do / does / did
> in emphatic form:
> He does work hard

> PRONUNCIATION
>
> • Silent letters
> • Emphatic stress on words

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Work in pairs to express opinion
> • Emphasize words in a sentence
> • ………………….   ?   ………………..
> • ………………….   ?   ………………..

**Lexique de la leçon** : *appeal, bullying, bustle, log on, texting* (MY NEW WORDS) ; *enrol,
bustling, side-effects, panic attacks, drawbacks* ; expressions d'accord/désaccord (I think… ; I'm
afraid I don't agree… ; I see what you mean, but… ; You are making a good point, however… ; What you
say is … but I still strongly believe…) ; mots à lettres muettes (*right, half, taught, friend,
bustle, psychology, knowledge, comb, knee, through, plumber, biscuits, harmful, autumn, listen*).

#### Lesson 4 — ON-LINE LEARNING (p.101–102)

- **Skills blend** : READING INTO WRITING (bandeau p.101)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading quotations
- **Communicating** : Writing — Punctuation ; Commas in apposition ; Writing a formal argumentative
  letter
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Expressing opinion
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.101) : trois citations **A**, **B**, **C** d'e-learners tirées du magazine **ESL
(English as a Second Language), June 2003** — A : le temps à consacrer à un cours en ligne est le
même qu'en présentiel, « if not more » ; B : certains étudiants croient l'e-learning plus facile
parce qu'ils n'ont pas à se déplacer ; C (support de l'exercice, avec ses pointillés imprimés) :
« Online learning, like all learning, requires a commitment of time. If you don't have enough time,
you can make more time. Some options are sleeping less, …….? ….. , …….? ……, ……….? ………., ………..?
………… and so on. » (p.102) Citation de **John Bears**, *author of Bears' Guide to Earning Degrees By
Distance Learning*, sur la flexibilité de l'enseignement à distance ; cadre de lettre à compléter
(« Dear Mr Bears, » + 12 lignes pointillées + ligne de signature).

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.101> **Reacting to quotations** — activité **1** : « This is what some e-learners think. about
   [sic] being a cyber student. What do you think? Read the three quotations from the ESL (English as
   a Second Language) magazine of June 2003 and check if you agree with these e-learners. »
2. <p.101> Activité **2** : « Focus on quotation C and complete it with more options to make more
   time. »
   *Clé (guide p.25)* : pas de corrigé fermé — « Quotation C would foster several alternative options
   for making more time. Done in groups, it will provide students with opportunities to exchange
   ideas and list the options they suggest individually then pool them into a group or class list to
   be shared by all. »
3. <p.101> **Using commas** : a) « In quotation C, 2 commas are used in the following phrase
   … , like all learning , » ; b) « John Bears, author of Bear's Guide to Earning Degrees by
   Distance Learning ,… ».
   *Note (guide p.25)* : « The reading activity leads the students into the use of commas when adding
   a phrase to another as an explanation. Quotation C, line 1, contains such commas before and after
   "like all learning". Reflecting on the use of commas will facilitate the understanding of the
   apposition in the next task. »
4. <p.102> **DEVELOP YOUR WRITING SKILL** : à partir de la citation de John Bears (« it offers a
   level of flexibility unavailable from a traditional program, in that the student can pursue his or
   her studies whenever and wherever he or she wishes. ») — « To which extent do you agree with this
   statement? Write a letter to Mr John Bears to explain your viewpoint and state the benefits or the
   drawbacks of distance learning. Remember to use **formal English** as you are addressing a person
   you do not know. Be tactful if you counter his valid point of view. Justify your opinion with
   convincing arguments. »
   *Note (guide p.25)* : « Make sure the students follow the steps of the writing process when
   producing the letter. Peer correction is very much recommended. You might want to help improve a
   sample letter by copying it for the whole class and getting everyone to edit it. Use the correction
   code in the "Check Year 3 Programme" section. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)**

> A comma ( , ) is used :
> • before and after a phrase in apposition , giving additional information
>    about the noun it follows. (An apposition is the addition of a phrase to
>    another as an explanation.)
>  • to separate items on a list.

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Use commas appropriately
> • Write a formal letter
> • Support my ideas with arguments
> • …………….. ? ………………..
>  What else ?

*(cette leçon n'a ni encadré MY NEW WORDS ni encadré MY GRAMMAR)*

**Lexique de la leçon** : *on-line learner, cyber student, e-learning, time commitment, distance
learning, apposition, comma, flexibility, benefits / drawbacks, tactful*.

---

#### Lesson 5 — COMPARING EDUCATIONAL SYSTEMS (p.103–106)

- **Skills blend** : SPEAKING INTO READING (bandeau p.103)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading — Interpreting data ; Analyzing information
- **Communicating** : Speaking — Discussing in pairs
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Comparing and contrasting
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.103) : encadré officiel « **The Republic Of Tunisia — Ministry of Education and
Training / THE NEW EDUCATION REFORM IN TUNISIA / AN EDUCATION STRATEGY FOR THE FUTURE 2002 - 2007** »
suivi de 4 puces (*Schooling which lasts 12 years* • *Schooling which is divided into 3 cycles
(primary / preparatory / secondary)* • *Compulsory education which lasts 9 years* • *Compulsory
education which is free of charge*) ; tableau du système américain (*6 till 11 — Elementary — Grades
1-6* / *12 till 14 — Junior High — Grades 7-9* / *15 till 18 — Senior High — Grades 10-12* / *Over 18
— College or University*). (p.104) Notice **Nouvelle-Zélande** ≈ 60 mots (scolarité obligatoire de 6
à 16 ans, droit jusqu'à 18, entrée à 5 ans, intermediate school, secondaire = 5 ans). (p.104)
**Education in England** : deux colonnes *Primary Phase* (Infant Schools 5-7, Junior Schools 7-11,
Primary Schools 5-11) / *Secondary Phase* (Secondary schools 11-16 ou 11-18, Sixth Form Colleges
16-18), deux paragraphes descriptifs (matières, GCSE, 3 « core » + 7 « foundation » subjects) et le
National Curriculum en quatre **Key Stages** (KS1 5-7, KS2 7-11, KS3 11-14, KS4 14-16). (p.105)
**Education in Australia** : 4 puces (13 ans de scolarité ; preparatory year + primary + secondary ;
obligation jusqu'à 15 ans ; 12.4 élèves par enseignant en moyenne) + encadré **School of the Air**
≈ 90 mots (radio, puis satellite, internet, téléphone, fax et poste pour les enfants isolés ;
première classe en 1951).

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.103> **IN PAIRS** — activité **1** : « Complete the information about the Tunisian educational
   system. Ask your teacher for help if you do not have the data. » Grille imprimée : *Total duration
   of schooling?* / *Number of cycles?* / *Compulsory education : 2 or 3 cycles?* / *Primary education
   lasts…… years.* / *Basic………* / *Basic (2nd cycle) ………* / *Secondary………*
2. <p.103> Activité **2** : « Now study the data about American education and see whether there are
   any similarities with Tunisia's system. » (tableau ci-dessus)
3. <p.103> Activité **3** : « Produce a table showing the stages of the Tunisian system. »
4. <p.103–104> Activité **4** : « Read the information about education in New Zealand then draw a
   Venn Diagram showing the similarities or the differences between the educational systems in
   **Tunisia** and in **New Zealand**. »
5. <p.104–105> **Interpreting data** : « Read the data about education in England and Australia. »
6. <p.105> Activité **1** : « Check whether there are any similarities between England and
   Australia. »
7. <p.105> Activité **2** : « Complete the Venn Diagram (the two intertwined circles) with the
   similarities and the differences between the 2 educational systems shown above. »
8. <p.105> Activité **3** : « Go round the class and check what the other groups have written. Show
   them your diagram and let them comment on it. »
9. <p.105> Activité **4** : « Present your diagram to the whole class. »
10. <p.105> Activité **5** : « Use connectors to compare and contrast the 2 systems. Use these
    linkers : **whereas – while – unlike – however** »
11. <p.106> « Read the information about **England** and answer the following questions. »
    **1.** How many types of teachers provide instruction to English pupils at different levels of
    learning? What are they called? **2.** What do you call the basic, common subjects at early
    secondary school? **3.** What is the national examination taken at the end of secondary
    education?
12. <p.106> « Read the information about **Australia** and » **1.** identify the special features of
    the Australian system. **2.** interpret the teacher-student ratio. Is it common in other
    countries? Justify your answer. **3.** comment upon the teachers' plan to reach the students who
    live in far away places. **4.** check the means used besides the radio network. Which connector
    justifies this?

*Clé (guide p.25)* : aucune réponse fournie pour la leçon 5 — seulement une note de conduite de
classe : « This lesson provides opportunities for discovery of different systems of education and
gets students to compare and discuss. Finding out about other people and countries is fundamental to
expand one's knowledge of the world and evaluate one's own context. If time allows, explore in depth
some of the details mentioned in the various systems. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.106

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Interpret data
> • Use a Venn diagram
> • Compare and contrast
> • …………. ? …………

*(cette leçon n'a ni encadré MY NEW WORDS ni encadré MY GRAMMAR)*

**Lexique de la leçon** : *cycle, compulsory, free of charge, elementary, junior high, senior high,
intermediate school, sixth form college, core subjects, foundation subjects, GCSE, Key Stage,
National Curriculum, teacher-student ratio, remote communities* ; linkers imposés *whereas – while –
unlike – however*.

#### Lesson 6 — AGE OR …. ? (p.107–110)

- **Skills blend** : READING INTO SPEAKING (bandeau p.107)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading a newspaper article
- **Communicating** : Speaking — Producing a speech
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Expressing satisfaction and certainty ; **Prepositions with
  verbs**
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.107) : **article de presse**, titre à compléter « School where pupils will be taught
according to ……………… instead of age », source imprimée « ***Daily Mail*, April 11th, 2005** »,
≈ 450 mots découpés en trois sections repérées **(A)**, **(B)**, **(C)** — Bridgemary School
(Gosport, Hampshire) devient la première *state school* d'Angleterre à regrouper tous ses élèves par
**aptitude** et non par âge : évaluation par tests et jugement des enseignants, cinq niveaux (access,
entry, level one, level two, level three), 1,100 élèves de 11 à 16 ans, zone urbaine défavorisée,
propos de la directrice Mrs Heron et du président du conseil d'administration Dr Faysal Mikdadi,
dispositif suivi par l'autorité locale et l'Ofsted. (p.108) Paragraphe détaché à réinsérer dans
l'article. (p.109) Texte lacunaire ≈ 150 mots sur la **langue des signes nicaraguayenne**. (p.110)
Encadré de 7 amorces pour préparer un discours.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.107> **IN PAIRS** — « Discuss with a partner, then share with the whole class. »
   **1.** What are classes like in Tunisian schools ? **2.** How are students grouped in classes?
   **3.** What about your own class. Is it made up of *outstanding* students only? **4.** Compare
   your school with pioneer schools. **5.** What does the Education Act say about schooling?
   *Clé (guide p.25–26)* : « 1. Classes are rather large with an average number of 35 students per
   class. 2. grouping per age. 3. Explain "outstanding" if you find out that students do not know it
   for some reason. Use *excellent* instead. Link the use of "outstanding" to pioneer schools that
   receive very good achievers. 4. Education is free and compulsory from the age of six to sixteen. »
   + « Get a copy of the Education Act from your school library or access the internet to find
   information about education in Tunisia. the edunet site provides such information. »
2. <p.107> « Read the title and suggest a word to complete it. » — *School where pupils will be
   taught according to ……………… instead of age*
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « Title : Age or **Ability** ».
3. <p.108> Activité **1** : « Explain how this school is different from other state schools. »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « 1. this school groups children by ability, not by age. »
4. <p.108> Activité **2** : « Complete the table. » — colonnes *Reason for starting this school* /
   *How students' level is chosen* / *Location* (« ………… in Hampshire »).
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : Reason = « A radical initiative to boost education » ; How = « Assessed via
   tests by teachers then allocated to one level according to their abilities » ; Location =
   « Deprived urban areas in Hampshire ».
5. <p.108> Activité **3** : « The following paragraph has been removed from the article. Which
   section does it belong to : (A), (B) or (C) ? » Paragraphe imprimé : « It means bright
   12-year-olds will be able to begin GCSE or even A-level courses with older pupils, while teenagers
   struggling with their reading, writing or maths will share classes with younger pupils.
   Headmistress Cheryl Heron said yesterday: "If the children are able, why stop them? »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « 3. the paragraph belongs to section B ».
6. <p.108> Activité **4** : « Find out the aim of the headmistress adopting this scheme. »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « 4. to help children achieve their potential. »
7. <p.108> Activité **5** : « Identify : a) the two categories of students the school distinguishes
   between. b) the five levels the students go through. »
8. <p.108> Activité **6** : « Pick out details showing the education authorities are satisfied with
   Mrs Heron's plan. »
9. <p.108> Activité **7** : « Find words meaning a) to increase the strength (section A) ;
   b) evaluated (section A) ; c) poor (section A) ; d) basic knowledge of arithmetic or mathematics
   in general (section B) ; e) I think that (Section B). »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « 7. a) boost - b) assessed - c) deprived – d) numeracy - e) I am of the
   opinion that. »
10. <p.108> Activité **8** : « Paraphrase this statement : « I care about whether these children are
    achieving their potential. » »
11. <p.108> Activité **9** : « Choose the adjective that best fits the statement. The chairman of
    governors at Bridgemary sounds (**boastful - indifferent - defiant**) when he says : « I could
    not care less about an ideal ». »
12. <p.109> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : « The following sentences are all from the article.
    Fill in the gaps with the right prepositions. » a) *… then allocated ------ one of the five
    levels.* b) *The new personalised system was aimed ------ tackling the culture of under
    achievement.* c) *Access deals ------ basic numeracy; level three is equivalent ------ A levels.*
    d) *It transformed traditional tutor groups ------ mixed age groups;* e) *I could not care less
    ------ an ideal.*
    *Clé* : non donnée par le guide (les prépositions sont lisibles dans l'article imprimé p.107).
13. <p.109> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **2** : « Group the words in the box below according to the
    prepositions they are used with. » Encadré : *succeed - depend - approve - deprived - prevent -
    suffer - independent - believe - differ - result.* Quatre cercles imprimés : **from**, **in**,
    **on**, **of**. « Add more verbs if you can. »
14. <p.109–110> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **3** : « Fill in each blank with the right preposition
    from the box. Some prepositions can be used twice. » Encadré : *among - for - at - from - into -
    of - to*. Texte support ≈ 150 mots sur la langue des signes nicaraguayenne (8 trous p.109 et
    3 trous p.110).
15. <p.110> **DEVELOP YOUR SPEAKING SKILL** : « Mrs Herson [sic] is invited to deliver a speech at an
    international conference on the alternative teaching approaches. Make her speak about her new plan
    and its aims. Use the information in the box below to prepare the speech. » Encadré de 7 amorces :
    *focus on ability rather than age* • *have mixed classes depending on student's academic
    achievement* • *it's high time we paved a quick way to high achievers* • *low achievers shouldn't
    be overlooked* • *lower failure rate* • *improve teaching methods* • *selection is beneficial for
    both good and less good students*.

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.110

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> assess, boost, numeracy, stream, switch, tackle

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> Prepositions
> to depend on
> to be independent of
> to suffer from

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Reinsert a paragraph where appropriate
> • Interpret an attitude
> • Prepare and deliver a speech
> • ……….? …………

**Lexique de la leçon** : *assess, boost, numeracy, stream, switch, tackle* (MY NEW WORDS) ;
*ability, deprived, personalised learning, under-achievement, streaming, comprehensive ideal,
Ofsted, GCSE, A-levels, literacy, potential* ; adjectifs d'attitude *boastful / indifferent /
defiant* ; verbes + prépositions (*succeed, depend, approve, deprived, prevent, suffer, independent,
believe, differ, result*).

---

#### Lesson 7 — LIFELONG LEARNING (p.111–114)

- **Skills blend** : SPEAKING INTO READING (bandeau p.111)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading — Recognizing text structure and outlining
- **Communicating** : Speaking — discussing a quote
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Expressing opinion ; **Affixation : -hood**
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.111) : proverbe « **Never too late to learn** » ; photo d'une conférence pour
adultes ; citation sur la popularité du *lifelong learning* auprès des responsables politiques ;
citation ≈ 55 mots sur l'éducation des adultes comme nécessité nationale permanente ; deux
spidergrammes *Advantages of lifelong learning* / *Disadvantages of lifelong learning*. (p.112)
**Quatre extraits sélectionnés de Lindeman, 1926**, numérotés 1 à 4 dans la page — 1 : l'éducation
n'est pas une simple préparation, la vie entière est apprentissage, d'où « education can have no
endings » ; 2 : l'éducation des adultes tourne autour d'idéaux non professionnels et commence là où
s'arrête la formation professionnelle ; 3 : on part des situations, non des matières — en éducation
des adultes le programme se construit autour des besoins de l'apprenant ; 4 : la ressource de plus
grande valeur est l'expérience de l'apprenant. (p.114) Paragraphe de 4 phrases en désordre sur les
trois traits du *lifelong education*.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.111> **Proverb Interpretation** : « Can you explain the following proverb? "Never too late to
   learn" » puis « Can you explain what is meant by 'lifelong' in the following statement? » (le
   statement imprimé : *Lifelong learning along with ideas such as 'the learning society' have become
   popular with politicians and policy makers in a number of countries. But what does it mean?*)
2. <p.111> **SPEAKING / IN PAIRS** — activité **1** : « Discuss the following. » Citation imprimée
   (≈ 55 mots) : l'éducation des adultes ne doit être ni un luxe pour quelques-uns ni l'affaire d'une
   courte période de la jeunesse ; elle est « a permanent national necessity, an inseparable aspect
   of citizenship, and therefore should be both universal and lifelong ». « What do **YOU** think? »
3. <p.111> Activité **2** : « Discuss the importance of lifelong learning. Try to complete the
   following spidergrams. Be ready to report to the whole class. » (*Advantages of lifelong
   learning* / *Disadvantages of lifelong learning*)
4. <p.112> **READING** — activité **1** : « Read the 4 extracts selected from Lindeman, 1926 and
   match them with the appropriate headings. » Intitulés imprimés : **A.** The learner's experience
   is paramount ; **B.** Adult education should not be only professional ; **C.** Education is for
   life ; **D.** We should start with interests and needs.
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « A. We must use the learner's experience → 4 ; B. Adult education should not
   be only professional → 2 ; C. Education is life → 1 ; D. We should start with situations not
   subjects → 3 » (le guide reformule les intitulés A et D, et ajoute un couple **E – c** qui n'a pas
   de correspondant imprimé — cf. § Incertitudes).
5. <p.113> Activité **2** : « Focus on the first extract and a) pick out a sentence expressing a
   cause – result relationship ; b) pick out words meaning almost the same as : *simply* / *
   consequenthy* [sic] / *limited* / *full growth and development*. »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « 2-b : simply : merely ; consequently : therefore ; limited : confined ;
   full growth and development : maturity. » (le a) n'est pas corrigé par le guide)
6. <p.113> Activité **3** : « Focus on the second extract and answer these questions. a) Should both
   forms of education (for a profession and for the sake of learning) occur simultaneously? b) What
   is meant by '**its**'? »
7. <p.113> Activité **4** : « Focus on the third extract. a) Determine whether these statements are
   true or false. Justify your choice : *Both situations and subjects are necessary in adult
   education.* / *In adult education, the learners programs cater for the learners' needs and
   interests.* b) Find words meaning almost the same as : *traditional* / *to adapt*. »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « 4.b conventional ; adjust ».
8. <p.113> Activité **5** : « Focus on the fourth extract and explain — how the learners' experience
   is a key element in adult education ; — find a word that means : *authentic*. »
9. <p.113> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : « What do you call the age when you are a) a child?
   b) a boy? c) an adult? d) a man? »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « a) childhood b) boyhood ; c) adulthood ; d) manhood ».
10. <p.113> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **2** : « Explain in your words : " a short span of early
    manhood" »
11. <p.114> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **3** : « Complete as appropriate. Your [sic] are given an
    example. *Being a woman is womanhood.* / Being a father is ……… / Being a brother is ……… / Being a
    sister is ……… »
    *Clé (guide p.26)* : « fatherhood ; brotherhood ; sisterhood. »
12. <p.114> Activité **4 — Checking coherence** : « The following sentences are not in the right
    order. Number them from 1 to 4 to get a coherent paragraph. What clues helped you ? » Phrases
    imprimées dans le désordre : *Three key features stand out of the accounts of lifelong
    education:* / *Last, it rests on the belief that individuals are, or can become, self-directing,
    and that they will see the value in engaging in lifelong education.* / *Then, it extends beyond
    the formal educational providers to encompass all agencies, groups and individuals involved in any
    kind of learning activity.* / *First, lifelong education is seen as building upon and affecting
    all existing educational providers, including both schools and institutions of higher education.*
    *Clé (guide p.26)* : ordre rétabli — « First, lifelong education is seen as building upon and
    affecting all existing educational providers, including both schools and institutions of higher
    education... Then, it extends beyond the formal educational providers to encompass all agencies,
    groups and individuals involved in any kind of learning activity... Last, it rests on the belief
    that individuals are, or can become, self-directing, and that they will see the value in engaging
    in lifelong education. (Tight 1996) ». Indices = les linkers de séquence *First / Then / Last*.

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.114

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> confined, maturity, merely, venture

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> Noun + -hood
>      noun
>
> motherhood
>
> Linkers of sequence

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Guess the meaning of unfamiliar words through context clues.
> • Match titles with paragraphs
> • Reorder sentences to get a coherent paragraph
> • …………..? …………………….
> Anything else?

**Lexique de la leçon** : *confined, maturity, merely, venture* (MY NEW WORDS) ; *lifelong learning,
adult education, vocational / non-vocational, conventional, adjust, genuine, paramount,
self-directing* ; dérivés en **-hood** (*childhood, boyhood, adulthood, manhood, womanhood,
motherhood, fatherhood, brotherhood, sisterhood*) ; linkers de séquence *First / Then / Last*.

---

#### Lesson 8 — READING THE BACK COVER OF A BOOK (p.115–117)

- **Skills blend** : aucun bandeau de blend imprimé sur la page d'ouverture — seul le bandeau
  **IN PAIRS** figure (p.115). Le UNIT OUTLINE ne renseigne que « Reading a book cover » (colonne
  *Communicating* vide).
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading a book cover
- **Communicating** : — (colonne vide au UNIT OUTLINE)
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Compound adjectives ; Affixation
- **Pronunciation** : Minimal pairs ; Syllable stress

**Supports** (p.115) : **deux quatrièmes de couverture** — **A** : *Which is Which?*, ouvrage classant
les mots par sonorité (homophones), avec transcription phonétique, orthographe, catégorie
grammaticale et exemples ; **B** : dictionnaire de poche pour apprenants (over 30.000 references ;
pronunciation at each entry ; up-to-date vocabulary ; includes American English ; help with grammar ;
help with irregular spellings ; many examples of usage). (p.116) **Quatre extraits critiques de la
quatrième de couverture d'un roman** (auteur non nommé), citant « piercingly-accurate visual detail »,
« one of the half-dozen best story writers in English today », « unforgettable characters …
brilliantly depicted », « the tough precision of poetry and the closely-observed naturalness of
everyday life », « an emotional genius so palpable one experiences it like a finger pressing steadily
upon the prose ».

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.115> **IN PAIRS** — activité **1** : « Read and complete with suitable words. » Texte à trous :
   « A ……… is a thick protective outer page of a book. There is a ……… cover and a back ……… . The title
   of the book and the name of the author(s) usually appear on ……… cover whereas the ……… cover
   provides information or ……… about ……… »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « A book cover is a thick protective outer page of a book. There is a **front**
   cover and a **back** cover. The title of the book and the name of the author(s) usually appear on
   the **front** cover whereas the **back** cover provides information or ……… about the content. »
   (le guide laisse lui aussi le trou de « information or ……… » non rempli)
2. <p.115–116> Activité **2** : « Read the following back covers and choose the appropriate
   alternative. » — « 'A' is the back cover of a : a) dictionary / **d)** book of homophones [sic —
   la lettre imprimée est *d* au lieu de *b*] / c) novel / d) history book » ; « 'B' is the back cover
   of a : a) pocket dictionary / **d)** book of homophones [sic] / c) thesaurus / d) cookery book ».
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « 2/ Back covers : A/ b (dictionary of homophones) ; B/ a (pocket
   dictionary) ».
3. <p.116> « Read these excerpts from the **back cover of a novel** and » activité **1** : « check
   what kind of information is provided for readers. »
4. <p.116> Activité **2** : « Read the whole page and identify 3 compound adjectives that are made up
   of an adverb and an adjective or a past participle. »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « Compound adjectives : piercingly-accurate ; brilliantly-depicted ;
   closely-observed. »
5. <p.116> Activité **3** : « Rephrase them in your own words to show you understand them. »
6. <p.116> Activité **4** : « Read the extracts and find words that mean : a) unfamiliar ; b) exact ;
   c) sharp ; d) six ; e) described ; f) memorable ; g) persons in a novel ; h) scenery. »
   *Clé (guide p.26)* : « 4. a-odd ; b-accurate ; c-acute ; d-half-dozen ; e-depicted ;
   f-unforgettable ; g-characters ; h-landscape. »
7. <p.117> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — « Read the back cover again. » **1.** Pick out 4 adverbs. How do they
   end? What do you notice? **2.** Provide the corresponding adjective forms. **3.** Identify the
   adjectives in the extract. Copy them in your notebook and supply the corresponding noun forms.
   **4.** What suffixes are used to form adjectives? List all the adjective endings you know :
   `…able; …al; ………….; ………….; ………….;………….;…………….;…………` **5.** Keep your adjectives, nouns and
   adverbs in a file inside your PORTFOLIO.
   *Clé* : non donnée par le guide.
8. <p.117> **PRONUNCIATION FOCUS** — activité **1** : « Read these words aloud and check if the
   underlined sounds are similar or different. » a) clo**s**ely / ob**s**erved ; b) tou**gh** /
   **g**enius ; c) pr**o**se / wh**o**se ; d) **ch**aracters / **c**orner ; e) st**ea**dily /
   inh**a**bit ; f) vi**s**ual / preci**si**on ; g) ra**ci**al / preci**si**on ; h) ven**tu**re /
   na**tu**ral.
   *Clé (guide p.27)* : « a : D ; b : D ; c : D ; d : S ; e : D ; f : S ; g : D ; h : S. »
   (D = different, S = similar)
9. <p.117> **PRONUNCIATION FOCUS** — activité **2** : « Which syllable is stressed in these words?
   Circle it. Example : **al**ways » — *accurate - precision - genius - compelling - experience -
   imaginative - implications - injustice - emancipation - emotional - inhabit - society -
   determined.*
   *Clé* : non donnée par le guide.

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.117

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> accurate, acute, characters, depicted, landscape, palpable, unforgettable

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> Compound adjectives
> Adverb + adjective or past participle
>
> piercingly-accurate

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Read the back cover of a book
> • ……………………….
> • ……………………….
> • ……………………….
> Complete as you wish

**Lexique de la leçon** : *accurate, acute, characters, depicted, landscape, palpable,
unforgettable* (MY NEW WORDS) ; *odd, half-dozen, inflection, naturalness, palpable, prose,
homophones, thesaurus, entry, phonetic spelling, part of speech* ; adjectifs composés
*piercingly-accurate, brilliantly-depicted, closely-observed*.

---

#### Lesson 9 — ALEXANDER GRAHAM BELL, THE…. (p.118–122)

- **Skills blend** : READING INTO WRITING (bandeau p.118)
- **Constructing meaning** : Speed Reading ; Extensive reading
- **Communicating** : Writing a summary
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Exclamation ; **The + adjective**
- **Pronunciation** : [ʃ]   [tʃ]

**Supports** (p.118–119) : **texte biographique en 6 paragraphes numérotés**, ≈ 700 mots, attribution
imprimée « **Adapted from *Twelve Famous Americans* (Compiled by Thomas Kral)** » — Alexander Graham
Bell, inventeur du téléphone, qui préférait être retenu comme professeur des sourds ; la famille Bell
et les arts de la communication (le grand-père élocutionniste à Édimbourg, auteur de *Stammering and
other Impediments of Speech* et de *The Practical Elocutionist*) ; le père, Alexander Melville Bell,
et son alphabet phonétique universel « **Visible Speech** » (34 symboles, 1864) ; les démonstrations
publiques et le commentaire du *London Illustrated Times* ; la mère musicienne et portraitiste,
devenue sourde alors qu'Alec avait douze ans ; la tournée américaine de 1868 et les débuts
professionnels d'Alec à Londres. (p.120) Photo de Bell au téléphone. (p.122) Encadré de 11 étapes de
résumé.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.118> Activité **1 — Check your reading rate** : « You are going to read a 6-paragraph text
   about Alexander Graham Bell, the inventor of the telephone. Take 1 minute to read as much as you
   can of the text. Stop after one minute sharp. Count the words you managed to read. What is your
   reading speed? Has it improved compared to your score when you read the biographical notes about
   Sting in the Introductory Unit? Go round the class and find out about your classmates' reading
   rate. What is the average number of words other students managed to read in one minute? »
   *Note (guide p.27)* : « Reading rate : Get the students to skim read as quickly as they can getting
   only the gist. »
2. <p.118> Activité **2** : « Once you have read the text, complete the title with ONE word (other
   than *inventor*). »
   *Clé (guide p.27)* : « 2. the teacher. »
3. <p.120> Activité **3** : « Read and find words and expressions that nearly mean the following :
   a) to be familiar with something (par.2) ; b) someone who writes a biography (par.1) ; c) people
   who are unable to speak or hear (par.1+4) ; d) handicaps (par.2) ; e) lack of luck (par.5) ;
   f) eternal / permanent (par.1+5) ; g) used all over the world (par.3). »
   *Clé (guide p.27)* : « 3. a- no stranger to ; b- biographer ; c- the deaf and dumb ;
   d- impediments ; e- misfortune ; f- timeless ; g- worldwide. »
4. <p.120> Activité **4** : « Answer the following questions : a) What qualities did Bell have as a
   teacher? Comment on the adjectives used and supply the corresponding noun forms. b) What shows
   that Bell's family were used to the arts of communication? Justify your answer with a phrase from
   the text. c) Who is the elder Bell? d) Who is the younger Alexander? Why is the comparative used?
   e) What's Bell's petname? f) What's Bell's birthplace? g) What are his mother's special talents?
   h) What's Bell's educational philosophy? »
5. <p.120> Activité **5** : « Focus on paragraph 2 and check Bell's age. a) Was he a grown up or a
   child? b) How old do you think he was then? c) What's meant by "older than his years" »
6. <p.120> Activité **6** : « Rephrase the following sentence in your own words. What does it
   express? What helped you understand it ? » — encadré « **What a teacher he was !** »
7. <p.120> Activité **7** : « Pick out a noun from each paragraph. Copy the 6 nouns in your notebook
   and supply the corresponding adjectives. »
8. <p.120> **LEXICAL FOCUS** — activité **1** : « Here are some synonyms of 2 words from paragraphs 1
   and 2. Find the 2 words. a) barrier, block, difficulty, hindrance, obstacle = ; b) ageless,
   changeless, endless, eternal, everlasting, permanent = . In which book are these synonyms to be
   found ? – An encyclopedia – A grammar book – A thesaurus »
   *Clé (guide p.27)* : « 1. a) impediment ; b) lasting. These synonyms can be found in a
   thesaurus. »
9. <p.120> **LEXICAL FOCUS** — activité **2** : « Choose a word from the reading and list its
   synonyms. Check them later in a thesaurus or a dictionary of synonyms and antonyms. »
10. <p.121> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : « *What a teacher he was !* Pick out the nouns that
    refer to Bell's family members and produce similar sentences about Bell's mother, father and
    grandfather. »
11. <p.121> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **2** : « *He would rather be remembered as a teacher of
    **the deaf** (Par.1).* What do you think **the deaf** means? Is it just one person or a group? »
12. <p.121> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **3** : « Complete the following : `The + ? is used to refer
    to a ? of people in society` » — exemples imprimés : *The poor (= poor people)* / *The old and the
    sick (= old people and sick people)* / *The blind (= blind people)*.
13. <p.121> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **4** : « Work in groups of 3 to sort out the following
    scrambled adjectives to get 3 lists. Put each adjective under the appropriate heading. Each member
    of the group will have a complete list to check with other groups' lists. » Encadré « **The** » :
    *rich, blind, elderly, homeless, young, strong, disabled, sick, old, hungry, deaf, unemployed,
    middle-aged*. Tableau à 3 colonnes × 4 lignes : *Social or Economic* / *Physical or Health* /
    *Age*.
    *Clé (guide p.27)* : « Social or Economic : the rich, the homeless, the hungry, the unemployed ;
    Physical or Health : the sick, the deaf, the disabled, the blind, the srong [sic — *strong*] ;
    Age : the middle-aged, the old, the young, the elderly. »
14. <p.121> **PRONUNCIATION FOCUS** : « Which three words do not belong in the group as they contain
    a different sound? Focus on the underlined letters. » — *misfor**tu**ne, musi**ci**an,
    reputa**ti**on, lec**tu**re, tea**ch**ing, flouri**sh**ed, educa**ti**on.*
    *Clé (guide p.27)* : « The 3 words are : misfortune, lecture, teaching. They contain a different
    sound. Ask the students to say all the words aloud and check the difference. »
15. <p.122> **DEVELOP YOUR WRITING SKILL** : « Can you sum up the text you have just read? Give it a
    try by following the steps indicated below. » 11 étapes imprimées : 1. Outline the reading by
    giving a title to each paragraph. 2. Develop each of the 6 titles into a topic sentence.
    3. Support each topic sentence with details. 4. Produce a concluding sentence for each paragraph.
    5. Link your sentences with suitable connectors to get coherent paragraphs. 6. Make sure your
    paragraphs make a good summary of the text. 7. Make the necessary changes to improve your summary.
    8. Show your summary to a classmate for revision. 9. Revise your summary in the light of your
    classmate's comments. 10. Check for punctuation, spelling, capitalization and layout. 11. Read
    your summary one more time to see if you are happy about it. Puis : « Keep it in your PORTFOLIO.
    Check how many pieces of writing your PORTFOLIO contains. Make a list of the writing task types
    you have completed so far. »
    *Note (guide p.27)* : « Make sure the 11 instructions are followed and respected by all students.
    The words in boldtype are extremely important. Words related to summary skills : outline, topic
    sentence, support, concluding sentence, link. Words related to the writing process : link,
    improve ; revise. LINK belongs to both aspects : summarising and steps of the process. Get the
    students to list the writing task types that represent the samples inserted in the PORTFOLIO. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.122

> MY NEW WORDS AND EXPRESSIONS
>
> biographer, deaf and dumb, impediments, misfortune, timeless,
> to be no stranger to something

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> – The + adjective used for a group of people
> e.g. The old = old people
> – The exclamative form.

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Outline a text
> • Summarize a text
> • Understand petnames such as: Bob for Robert
>   Betty for Elizabeth
>   Bill for William
> What else?
> ………?..............
> ………?..............

**Lexique de la leçon** : *biographer, deaf and dumb, impediments, misfortune, timeless, to be no
stranger to something* (MY NEW WORDS AND EXPRESSIONS) ; *elocutionist, stammering, lisping, speech
defects, Visible Speech, vocal organs, phonetic alphabet, adept, to alleviate, worldwide, lasting* ;
adjectifs substantivés (*the rich, the poor, the homeless, the hungry, the unemployed, the sick, the
deaf, the disabled, the blind, the strong, the middle-aged, the old, the young, the elderly*) ;
lexique du résumé (*outline, topic sentence, support, concluding sentence, link, revise, improve*).

#### PROJECT WORK 2 — EXPOSITORY TEXTS (p.123)

*(La p.122 est la fin de la Lesson 9 — DEVELOP YOUR WRITING SKILL + encadrés de fin de leçon. Le
PROJECT WORK tient sur la seule p.123, sous le titre imprimé « PROJECT WORK 2 ».)*

- **Constructing meaning** : Expository texts (UNIT OUTLINE)

**Supports** (p.123) : encadré de 15 types de textes expositifs — *ads, biographies, brochures,
cartoons, definitions, interviews, invitations, lab reports, letters, lists, maps, directions, book
reports, editorials, newspaper articles*.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.123> **A.** activité **1** : « Which types do you already know? Tick them. »
2. <p.123> **A.** activité **2** : « Underline the ones you have come across in your new book (*Skills
   for Life*). Refer to the lesson(s) containing such types. »
3. <p.123> **A.** activité **3** : « Circle the ones you have never seen. »
4. <p.123> **A.** activité **4** : « Check which types of expository texts : *analyse – inform –
   explain – describe – persuade – define – classify – compare* »
   *Clé (guide p.28)* : « Newspaper articles, editorials, reports, lab reports : analyse, inform,
   describe, persuade, classify, explain, compare. Brochures, ads, lists, directions, maps,
   definitions, biographies : inform, describe, define, compare, explain, classify. Show students how
   each text can have several functions and purposes. »
5. <p.123> **A.** activité **5** : « Supply the noun form of the eight verbs in Question 4. »
   *Clé (guide p.28)* : « 5. Noun forms : analysis, persuasion, information, definition, explanation,
   classification, description, comparison. »
6. <p.123> **A.** activité **6** : « Circle the stressed syllable in each of the 16 words (8 verbs
   and 8 nouns) »
   *Clé (guide p.28)* : « 6. Check a dictionary for noun and verb stress. »
7. <p.123> **A.** activités **7** et **8** : « Check your answers with other classmates and with your
   teacher. » / « Insert your work in your PORTFOLIO for future use. »
8. <p.123> **B. WEBQUEST** : « Use the Google search engine. Type in the phrase "expository texts" to
   see samples. Select the sample text you like best. Try to produce a similar text which you will
   keep in your PORTFOLIO. »

*Note (guide p.28)* : « This has a text type focus to familiarise students with expository texts and
their functions. »

**Lexique** : *expository text, ad, biography, brochure, cartoon, definition, interview, invitation,
lab report, letter, list, map, directions, book report, editorial, newspaper article* ; couples
verbe/nom *analyse–analysis, persuade–persuasion, inform–information, define–definition,
explain–explanation, classify–classification, describe–description, compare–comparison*.

#### For Arts Students : Session 3 — LATER, by MICHAEL FOSTER (p.124–127)

- **Public** : ⚠️ Arts students uniquement (section Lettres — 5 h/semaine ; hors tronc commun)
- **Skills blend** : EXTENSIVE READING (bandeau p.124)
- **Constructing meaning** : Extensive reading of a short story
- **Communicating** : —
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : **Reporting verbs**
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.124–126) : **nouvelle « Later » de Michael Foster**, ≈ 1 300 mots, imprimée en
quatre parties dont les intitulés sont à retrouver (**A.** ____ / **B.** ____ / **C.** ____ /
**D.** ____). Résumé : John Carmody, chez lui un après-midi de mardi, ne parvient à se rappeler ni
ses grands projets ni ses espoirs — seule lui revient une petite phrase de sa fille Marge. Quelques
semaines plus tôt, absorbé par un rapport annuel d'actionnaires, il avait éconduit l'enfant venue lui
demander de lui lire une histoire dans un livre à couverture verte ; elle avait fini par poser le
livre en disant qu'il pourrait la lire pour lui-même, « seulement assez fort pour qu'elle entende
aussi ». On comprend en fin de récit que Marge a été tuée par un chauffard à moitié ivre, aujourd'hui
en prison pour homicide involontaire, et que Carmody, sa femme prête pour l'enterrement, lit enfin le
conte à voix haute. *(Texte non reproduit — droits, R-2.)*

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.124> **EXTENSIVE READING** — activité **1** : « Read the story and place these headings relating
   to the different parts in the space provided. » Intitulés imprimés : a) Development of the drama ;
   b) Setting the scene for the story ; c) Conclusion ; d) Focus on the story ; e) Introducing the
   main characters, setting events in motion.
   *Clé (guide p.28)* : « 1/ A – b / B – e / C – d / D – a / **E – c** » — le guide corrige cinq
   emplacements alors que le manuel n'en imprime que quatre (cf. § Incertitudes).
2. <p.126> Activité **2** : « Complete the spidergram. » Deux spidergrammes imprimés : *characters*
   (3 bulles vides) et *Setting:* → *Time :* / *Place :*.
3. <p.126> Activité **3** : « Match the words with their synonyms or definitions. » 1. queer /
   2. to crumble / 3. rationally / 4. manslaughter / 5. anguish / 6. jail ↔ a) logically ; b) deep
   suffering ; c) killing someone ; d) prison ; e) to be destroyed ; f) strange.
   *Clé (guide p.28)* : « 3/ 1- f / 2- e / 3- a / 4- c / 5- b / 6- d ».
4. <p.127> Activité **4** : « The following statements are false. Say why. a) Tuesday was a cheerful
   day for John Carmody. b) Carmody read a fairy tale to his daughter. c) Marge was angry when her
   father said he was busy. d) The punk kid was in jail for drunken-driving. »
5. <p.127> Activité **5** : « Who do the following adjectives apply to? Put a tick where appropriate. »
   Tableau *Father / Daughter* × *regretful, polite, well-mannered, sad, busy*.
   *Clé (guide p.28)* : Regretful → Father ; Polite → Daughter ; Well-mannered → Daughter ; Sad →
   Father ; Busy → Father.
6. <p.127> Activité **6** : « Why does the father feel so guilty? »
7. <p.127> Activité **7** : « Who is to blame in this story? The father? The punk kid? Both? Justify
   your answer. »
8. <p.127> Activité **8** : « What do you think the moral of this story is? »
9. <p.127> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** : « When we report utterances we use reporting verbs like : **ask,
   advise, apologize, urge, beg, warn, invite, remind.** Read the following sentences from the story
   then report them using the verbs above. a) "Look, Daddy." b) "Will you read me a story in it?"
   c) "I'm sorry but I'm busy." d) "The story looks so exciting, Daddy." »
   *Clé* : non donnée par le guide.

*Note (guide p.28, « Hints »)* : « Explain to students that a narrative is composed of the following
elements : Setting the scene for the story / Introducing the main characters, Setting events in
motion / Focusing on the story / Developing the drama / Concluding. Show them that to write a
narrative they have to develop those elements. – Students should choose vocabulary carefully to
create the appropriate atmosphere – Use the past continuous or the simple past – Use direct speech to
make a narrative more immediate and dramatic – Make the final sentence of each paragraph prepare the
reader for the development of the story in the next part – Think of a suitable title. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim)** — p.127

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> anguish, crumble, jail, manslaughter, queer, rationally

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> Using reporting verbs
> advise, apologize, urge, beg, warn, invite, remind.

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Read a short story
> • Identify characters' profile
> • Identify the moral of a story
> •  …………? ………………

**Lexique de la leçon** : *anguish, crumble, jail, manslaughter, queer, rationally* (MY NEW WORDS) ;
verbes de compte rendu *ask, advise, apologize, urge, beg, warn, invite, remind* ; adjectifs de
profil *regretful, polite, well-mannered, sad, busy* ; lexique du récit (*setting, characters, drama,
conclusion, moral*).

#### For Arts Students : Session 4 — WRITING A NARRATIVE (p.128)

- **Public** : ⚠️ Arts students uniquement (section Lettres — 5 h/semaine ; hors tronc commun)
- **Skills blend** : — (aucun bandeau de blend imprimé)
- **Constructing meaning** : — (colonne vide au UNIT OUTLINE)
- **Communicating** : Writing a narrative for the PORTFOLIO
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : — (colonne vide au UNIT OUTLINE)
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.128) : encadré des 5 éléments constitutifs d'un récit + renvoi imprimé « (For more
detailed information about narrative texts, refer back to **Lesson 9, Unit 1**) ».

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.128> Rappel imprimé : « A narrative is composed of the following elements : 1. Setting the
   scene for the story ; 2. Introducing the main characters, setting events in motion ; 3. Focusing on
   the story ; 4. Developing the drama ; 5. Concluding. »
2. <p.128> Activité **1** : « Develop a five-paragraph narrative. Follow the instructions below :
   - Think of a suitable title. - Choose vocabulary carefully to create the appropriate atmosphere.
   - Use direct speech to make your narrative more immediate. - Make the final sentence of each
   paragraph prepare the reader for the development of the story in the next. »
3. <p.128> Activité **2** : « Revise your draft to check for grammar, vocabulary, spelling,
   punctuation and capitalization. »
4. <p.128> Activité **3** : « Read out the first paragraph for the whole class to comment on it. »
5. <p.128> Activité **4** : « Respond to the comments by justifying your choices. »
6. <p.128> Activité **5** : « Insert your final version of the narrative in your PORTFOLIO for your
   teacher to look at and provide recommendations. »
7. <p.128> Activité **6** : « Add this type of writing to the list you made when you checked your
   PORTFOLIO. »

*Note (guide p.28)* : « Have the students develop the narrative step by step. Make them revise their
draft to check for grammar, vocabulary, spelling, punctuation and capitalization, and read out the
first paragraph for the whole class to comment on it. Make sure they insert their final version of
the narrative in their PORTFOLIO. »

*(cette session n'a aucun encadré de fin — pas de MY NEW WORDS / MY GRAMMAR / WHAT I CAN DO NOW)*

**Lexique de la session** : *narrative, setting, main characters, drama, conclusion, atmosphere,
direct speech, draft, capitalization*.

#### CHECK YOUR LANGUAGE AND SKILLS 1 (p.129–133)

*(p.129 : page-titre pleine page « CHECK YOUR LANGUAGE AND SKILLS 1 », sans exercice. Unité de
consolidation en trois volets — CHECK YOUR READING SKILL, CHECK YOUR LANGUAGE, CHECK YOUR SKILLS /
READ and WRITE.)*

##### CHECK YOUR READING SKILL (p.130–131)

**Support** : article de presse « **A FOREIGN STUDENT IN BRITAIN** », source imprimée « ***The
Guardian*, Tuesday October 31, 2006** », ≈ 330 mots en 6 paragraphes numérotés + un chapeau
d'introduction et une photo. Sujet : Hoda Baraka, 23 ans, du Caire, inscrite en MSc *politics of the
world economy* à la London School of Economics, compare les systèmes universitaires américain et
britannique (attractivité académique de Londres, évaluation sur la force de l'argumentation plutôt
que sur les connaissances accumulées, vitalité culturelle de la capitale, masters d'un an contre
temps partiel, coût des études et rareté des bourses pour étrangers).

1. <p.130> Activité **1** : « Read the introduction and complete the table. » Tableau imprimé
   *Who? / What ? / Where ?* (3 cases vides).
2. <p.131> Activité **2** : « Read the text and tick the correct alternative. The text is mainly about
   a) Difficulties in an Egyptian student's life ; b) Studies in the USA and the UK ; c) International
   students in the UK »
   *Clé (guide p.29)* : « 2- c ».
3. <p.131> Activité **3** : « Find words in the text meaning a) a big choice (par.2) ; b) attract
   attention to (par.3) ; c) make it possible for someone to do something (par.4) ; d) persuade
   someone not to do something (par.5) ; e) providing information and understanding (par.6). »
   *Clé (guide p.29)* : « 3- a- a wide range of / b- highlight / c- enable / d- dissuade /
   e- enlightening ».
4. <p.131> Activité **4** : « "The financial headaches of studying here must be addressed" means :
   a) Journalists must write about the financial difficulties ; b) The government must think about and
   deal with the financial difficulties ; c) Students must fill in an application form not to have
   financial problems. »
   *Clé (guide p.29)* : « 4- b ».
5. <p.131> Activité **5** : « Do you think that the exposure to different cultures and mindsets is
   important for students? »
6. <p.131> Activité **6** : « Read this sentence from the text : "While the US system exposes students
   to a wide range of subject, the British system focuses on building critical thinking." What does
   the sentence express? a) Regret ; b) Contrast ; c) Suggestion »
   *Clé (guide p.29)* : « 6. b Contrast ».

##### CHECK YOUR LANGUAGE (p.131–132)

7. <p.131> Activité **1** : « Report these utterances beginning as shown. » Énoncé imprimé : *"I'm
   getting tired of waiting," he complained. "I've been sitting here for half an hour. The same thing
   happened when I came here yesterday. The next time it happens, I shall report it."* Amorces :
   « He complained that ……… because he ……… He added that ………, and ended by saying that ……… »
   *Clé (guide p.29)* : « 1. He complained that he was getting tired of waiting because he had been
   sitting there for half an hour. He added that the same thing had happened when he had come there
   the day before and ended by saying that the next time it happens, he shall report it. »
8. <p.132> Activité **2** : « Complete the text with words from the box. » Encadré : *tours, gifts,
   accommodation, enjoyable, friendly, provide, qualified, range*. Texte support : dépliant
   touristique « **Welcome to Cheltenham Spa** » (Tourist Information Centre — services proposés,
   réservation d'hébergement, billetterie, Scenic Coach Tours of the Cotswolds, visites à pied de
   Regency Cheltenham, cartes et guides), 7 trous.
   *Clé (guide p.29)* : « 2. qualified - enjoyable - accommodation - provide - tours - friendly -
   gifts. » (*range*, déjà imprimé dans « an extensive range », est l'intrus)

##### CHECK YOUR SKILLS — READ and WRITE (p.133)

**Support** : **poème « Youth Thoughts » de Jawida Ben Afia**, présenté comme issu de
**www.poetry.com**, « a poetry site that publishes winning poems submitted by poets from all over
the world and assessed by the International Library of Poetry specialists ». Sujet imprimé : « The
poem - Youth Thoughts - deals with young people's attitude to life. » — 14 vers en 4 strophes, très
majoritairement rimés en *-ay*. *(Texte non reproduit — droits, R-2.)*

9. <p.133> Activité **1** : « Read it and a) identify the verbs used. b) identify the words that
   express youth attitude. »
10. <p.133> Activité **2** : « Focus on out [sic] the last words of the first 8 lines. Identify the
    rhyme. »
11. <p.133> Activité **3** : « Read the poem aloud to practise the **[ei]** sound. »
12. <p.133> **How creative are you? / How good a POET are you?** — « • Can you write a poem? Choose a
    rhyme you would finish all the lines with. • Read your poem to the class. • What about submitting
    YOUR OWN poem for assessment? YOU may get a PRIZE. » puis « Access www.poetry.com to submit your
    poem and see lots of other poems that have won prizes. »
    *Note (guide p.29)* : « READ and WRITE aims to encourage students to write creatively and think of
    some praise and a prize. This fosters their inspiration and makes them feel eager to win. From
    reading INTO writing, students will have been exposed to a sample which they will imitate.
    Exposure to features of poetry such as rhymes enables learners to focus on format and overcome any
    constraints in vocabulary choice, rhythm, rhyme, verse and content adequacy. »

**Lexique de la section** : *a wide range of, highlight, enable, dissuade, enlightening, financial
outlay, scholarship, constraints, mindsets, graduate / undergraduate / postgraduate* ; lexique du
dépliant touristique (*qualified, enjoyable, accommodation, provide, tours, friendly, gifts*) ;
discours indirect (verbes introducteurs *complain, add, end by saying*) ; lexique de la poésie
(*rhyme, verse, line, [ei] sound*).

#### Incertitudes (Unit 2)

1. **Bornes de l'unité vs. bornes de la tranche.** La tranche demandée va jusqu'à la p.134, mais la
   p.134 est la **page de garde de l'UNIT 3** (« CREATIVE, INVENTIVE MINDS » — vérifié par rendu
   PNG). Le contenu d'Unit 2 s'arrête à la p.133. De même, la p.129 est une page-titre sans exercice
   et la p.122 appartient à la Lesson 9 (le PROJECT WORK 2 ne tient que sur la p.123).
2. **Lesson 7, activité 1 (p.112) — le guide corrige un intitulé qui n'est pas celui du manuel.**
   Le manuel imprime « **A.** The learner's experience is paramount » et « **D.** We should start with
   interests and needs » ; le guide (p.26) écrit « A. We must use the learner's experience » et
   « D. We should start with situations not subjects ». Les appariements (A→4, B→2, C→1, D→3) restent
   cohérents ; seuls les libellés divergent.
3. **For Arts Session 3, activité 1 (p.124–126) — cinq réponses pour quatre emplacements.** Le manuel
   n'imprime que quatre intitulés à placer (**A.** p.124, **B.** p.124, **C.** p.125, **D.** p.126 —
   vérifié par rendu PNG de la p.126) alors qu'il propose cinq étiquettes (a→e) et que le guide (p.28)
   donne cinq couples, dont « **E – c** » (Conclusion). Soit un emplacement **E** manque à
   l'impression, soit l'étiquette c) « Conclusion » est volontairement une intruse ; la source ne
   permet pas de trancher.
4. **Lesson 9, activité 4 (p.121) — 13 adjectifs pour 12 cases.** Le tableau imprimé offre 3 colonnes
   × 4 lignes (12 cases) tandis que l'encadré contient 13 adjectifs ; le guide (p.27) en place bien 13
   (dont **5** sous « Physical or Health »). Le tableau du manuel est donc trop court d'une ligne.
5. **Coquilles de la source relevées telles quelles** (`[sic]`) : « Fill in the diagram with the 3
   consequences **of**. » (p.90, consigne inachevée) ; les alternatives numérotées « a) d) c) d) »
   (p.116, deux fois) ; « consequenthy » (p.113) ; « Mrs **Herson** » pour Mrs Heron (p.110) ;
   « Your are given an example » (p.114) ; « This is what some e-learners think. about being a cyber
   student » (p.101) ; « Focus on **out** the last words » (p.133) ; côté guide : « Thisn » et « Itd »
   (p.24), « the **srong** » pour *the strong* (p.27), « enrollment » là où le manuel écrit
   *enrolment* (p.24 vs p.96), et « Bear's Guide » (p.101) vs « Bears' Guide » (p.102) pour le même
   ouvrage.
6. **Symboles phonétiques du UNIT OUTLINE (p.87).** La couche texte rendait `[0 ] [ t0 ]` ; le rendu
   PNG à 150 dpi confirme **[ʃ]** et **[tʃ]**.
7. **Activités sans corrigé dans le guide** (donc non transcrites en clé, aucune réponse inventée) :
   L1 act.2 des spidergrammes ; L2 act.1 (appariement acronymes) et act.2 du GRAMMAR FOCUS (7 items
   If/Unless) ; toute la Lesson 5 ; L6 GRAMMAR FOCUS 1-3 (prépositions) ; L7 act.2a et act.3 ; L8
   GRAMMAR FOCUS 1-5 et PRONUNCIATION FOCUS 2 ; L9 act.4 à 7 ; For Arts S3 GRAMMAR FOCUS (reporting
   verbs) ; CHECK YOUR READING SKILL act.1 et 5.
8. **Le tableau de la p.94** (chiffres régionaux) n'est pas corrigé par le guide ; les valeurs
   restituées dans cette fiche sont **lues directement dans l'extrait UIS imprimé au-dessus du
   tableau** — elles sont donc de la transcription, pas de la génération.
9. **Document sonore de la Lesson 3** (Part 1 / Part 2) : le script n'est pas dans la KEY de l'Unit 2 ;
   il figure dans le tapescript du guide (hors de cette tranche). Seules les réponses attendues,
   données par le guide p.25, sont reportées ici.
10. **Roman de la Lesson 8 (p.116)** : ni le titre ni l'auteur du roman dont sont tirés les quatre
    extraits critiques ne sont imprimés — le manuel ouvre les citations par « " …… has always had a
    sharp eye… " », le nom étant volontairement masqué.
