#### Lesson 1 — INVENTIONS RELATED WORDS (p.136–141)

- **Skills blend** : READING INTO WRITING (bandeau p.136)
- **Constructing meaning** : Dictionary skills ; Defining
- **Communicating** : — (colonne vide ; le titre porte « Webquest (Robots) »)
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Word form ; `Used to + V` ; `Be used to + V-ing`
- **Pronunciation** : /s/ /z/

**Supports** (p.136) : trois proverbes ; extrait de page de thésaurus pour l'entrée *invent* ;
encadré de définitions *create / invent / discover / find out*. (p.137) page de dictionnaire
*create / creation / creative* (entrées complètes avec transcriptions et exemples) + tableau de
famille de mots ; 10 fragments de phrases mélangés. (p.138) citation encadrée sur les robots ;
texte « CHECK YOUR WORD FORMS » (≈ 10 lignes, 9 mots entre crochets) ; capture d'écran d'un
moteur de recherche (Google Search / I'm Feeling Lucky / Advanced Search / Preferences /
Language Tools). (p.139) tableau à deux colonnes *Terms* (11 termes Internet) / *Definitions*
(11 définitions a→k). (p.140) page de dictionnaire de l'entrée *use* (use v., use n., used adj.,
used to modal verb, useful, usefulness, useless, user).

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.136> **READING INTO WRITING** — activité **1** : « Paraphrase these proverbs. » Trois
   proverbes imprimés : « A brain is an apparatus with which we think we think. » / « Necessity
   is the mother of invention. » / « Genius= 1% inspiration + 99% perspiration. »
   *Clé (guide p.30)* : « Proverb 3 should read : Genius is one per cent inspiration and
   ninety-nine per cent perspiration. »
2. <p.136> activité **2** : « Study this extract from a thesaurus page then determine which verbs
   have a stronger meaning than the other equivalents. Follow the example : fancy has a stronger
   meaning than imagine. » Extrait imprimé : `invent - Syn. devise, discover, create, find,
   fashion, form, fabricate, design, plan, frame, project, scheme, carry out, imagine, simulate,
   visualize, fancy, conceive.`
   *Clé (guide p.30)* : « conceive is stronger than devise ; scheme is stronger than plan ».
3. <p.136> activité **3** : « Study the examples in the box below and check the use of each verb.
   Would you have used other verbs from the thesaurus page instead? Try to explain some more
   verbs. » Encadré imprimé (verbatim) :
   > create / invent / discover / find out
   >
   > If you create something, you make something happen or exist.
   > if you invent something, you make or design something that did not exist before.
   > If you discover something, you find something that already exists.
   > You can find something out either by chance or by study or inquiry.
4. <p.137> activité **4** : « Read the dictionary page below and complete the table. » Tableau de
   famille de mots à 5 lignes : *Uncountable noun* → `creation` (donné) ; *Countable noun* →
   `creation` (donné) ; *Verb* → à compléter ; *Adverb* → à compléter ; *Adjective* → à compléter.
   (attendu d'après la page de dictionnaire imprimée : create / creatively / creative —
   *creativity* y figure aussi comme nom indénombrable).
5. <p.137> activité **5** : « These are scrambled sentences that make up definitions of what a
   human being is and what a robot is. Sort them out to get two short, coherent paragraphs. »
   Amorces graphiques imprimées : `A human being is …` et `A robot is …`. Fragments :
   a) a soft weak creature. b) never sleeps, c) it has to spend one third of its life asleep,
   d) and can work in any temperature. e) than human beings. f) It uses electrical energy
   directly, g) and it can't work if the temperature is too hot or too cold. h) It is stronger,
   more efficient i) made of strong metal. j) It needs constant supplies of air, water, and food;
   Puis (p.138) : « What clues helped you make up your definitions? »
   *Clé (guide p.30)* : « Definition 1 : A human being is a soft weak creature. It needs constant
   supplies of air, water and food. and it can't work if the temperature is too hot or too cold;
   it has to spend one third of its life asleep. — Definition 2 : A robot is made of strong metal.
   It is stronger, more efficient than human beings. It uses electrical energy directly, never
   sleeps and can work in any temperature. »
6. <p.138> activité **6** : « What do YOU think of robots' role(s) in our lives now and in the
   future? » avec la citation encadrée : « … Robots were first developed to work where humans
   found the work dangerous and hard. But the next generation of robots will co-exist with
   humans. »
7. <p.138> **WEBQUEST** (Webquest 5 : Robots) : chercher « Robots », trouver les dernières
   inventions du domaine, choisir un robot précis, rassembler et trier l'information, préparer un
   exposé pour le *school Technology club* — dire comment le robot fonctionne et à quoi il sert.
8. <p.138> **CHECK YOUR WORD FORMS** — texte à trous : 9 mots entre crochets à mettre à la forme
   qui convient : `[grant]`, `[run]`, `[invent]`, `[discover]`, `[continuous]`, `[collaborate]`,
   `[entire]`, `[essential]`, `[solve]`.
   *Clé (guide p.30)* : granted – running – inventing – discovering – continuously –
   collaboration – entirely – essentially – solving.
9. <p.138–139> **HOW FAMILIAR ARE YOU WITH INTERNET TERMS?** — a) apparier chaque terme à son
   explication. **Termes** : 1. attachment / 2. browser / 3. download / 4. emoticon / 5. FAQs /
   6. hypertext / 7. ICT / 8. netiquette / 9. portal / 10. search engine / 11. spamming.
   **Définitions** : a) copy something from another computer to your own ; b) Frequently Asked
   Questions ; c) a set of conventions for polite behaviour on the Internet ; d) a document sent
   along with an email message ; e) a program that allows you to read documents on the web ;
   f) a programme which allows users to search for specific information ; g) a symbol used to
   convey an emotion, a smiley : ; h) sending the same email to multiple lists, usually to
   advertise a product or service, which is against netiquette ; i) Information and Communication
   Technologies ; j) text in which links are inserted to allow the reader to read in a non-linear
   way ; k) a site which provides a 'gateway' into a range of Web services. Well-known portal
   sites include Yahoo! Alta Vista!
   Puis b) « Which terms did you know before reading the list? » c) « Which ones have you never
   seen? » d) « How clear is the meaning of all the terms? »
   *Clé (guide p.30)* : 1-d, 2-e, 3-a, 4-g, 5-b, 6-j, 7-i, 8-c, 9-k, 10-f, 11-h.
10. <p.139> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — **A. Answer the questions below.** 8 questions (les formes
    *used / use to* sont encadrées en couleur dans les questions 5 à 8) : 1) Did people use to use
    the Internet 20 years ago? Why? 2) How often do you access the Internet? 3) Are you getting
    familiar with it? 4) Do you know the percentage of Tunisian People who use the Internet?
    5) Do you think Tunisian students are now **used to** access**ing** the Internet? 6) Are you
    **used to** search**ing** information on the web? Do you find it easy? 7) How did you **use to**
    find information? 8) Which references did you **use** to get information?
11. <p.140> **GRAMMAR FOCUS — B.** 1) « Check the meaning of the verb *use* in the dictionary
    page. » (page de dictionnaire imprimée, 8 entrées). 2) « Focus on questions 5, 6, and 7 in A
    and comment on the different uses of the verb *use*. » 3) « Answer these questions : » a) How
    many entries are there on the dictionary page? b) What do you call someone who uses something?
    c) Explain what a username is. d) Identify the entries that contain suffixes. e) Identify 2
    antonyms. f) How many adjectives are there on the dictionary page? g) Which word(s) is(are)
    uncountable? Why? h) How easy is it for you to exploit dictionary entries?
12. <p.140–141> **PRONUNCIATION FOCUS** — activités **1** à **4** : 1) repérer, sur les
    transcriptions phonétiques de la page de dictionnaire, comment se prononce la lettre *-s-*.
    2) « How do you pronounce the underlined words below? Read them aloud. » — u**sed** to do
    something / to be u**sed** to doing something / to u**se** something / the u**se** of something
    / u**se**ful / u**se**less. 3) « Which sounds are similar? Which are different? » a) used to
    b) the use c) to use d) to be used to. 4) a) lire à voix haute 4 phrases en se concentrant sur
    le son /s/ : « I have never used a thesaurus. I often use dictionaries. I am used to accessing
    the World Wide Web. I am using several sources of information. » b) « What do you notice? How
    is the (s) pronounced? » c) fabriquer des phrases personnelles avec *use* montrant la
    différence entre /s/ et /z/.
    *Clé (guide p.30)* : « Pronunciation — 3. a and b are similar ; c and d are similar.
    4. b — All the words contain the sound /z/. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim, p.141)**

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> attachment , browser download, emoticon, FAQ hypertext, ICT, netiquette portal ,
> search engine spamming

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> Used to+V
> Be used to doing something

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Use a dictionary and a thesaurus
> • Understand and use Internet vocabulary appropriately
> • Search information on the web and present it in a talk
> • Pronounce different sounds of the same letter
> .................. ? ……………..
> .................. ? ……………..
> .................. ? ……………..

**Lexique de la leçon** : attachment, browser, download, emoticon, FAQ(s), hypertext, ICT,
netiquette, portal, search engine, spamming ; famille *create / creation / creative / creatively
/ creativity* ; synonymes de *invent* (devise, discover, create, find, fashion, form, fabricate,
design, plan, frame, project, scheme, carry out, imagine, simulate, visualize, fancy, conceive) ;
*grant, run, invent, discover, continuous(ly), collaborate/collaboration, entire(ly),
essential(ly), solve*.

#### Lesson 2 — TECHNOLOGY : A BLESSING IN DISGUISE? (p.142–146)

- **Skills blend** : SPEAKING INTO READING (bandeau p.142), sous-bandeau **IN PAIRS**
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading an article
- **Communicating** : Speaking — Expressing one's opinion
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : The Present Perfect
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.142) : paragraphe de presse encadré « **Privacy Fears Intensified By Tech That
Knows Where You Are** », attribution imprimée *National Geographic. (Oct.20, 2006)*, ≈ 10 lignes,
avec 8 mots soulignés à remplacer (identify, place, expanding, immediately, exactly, babyhood,
number, disturbing). (p.143) schéma à flèches à compléter (`AS ……` + axe `– …… +`).
(p.144) article « **The iPod : Any Future?** » — sous-titre imprimé « Yes, the omnipresent music
player is a powerful force. But what comes next? » — attribution *Special Report by Michael
Rogers, Columnist, Nov. 8, 2006* ; 4 paragraphes numérotés, ≈ 30 lignes, deux coupures signalées
`[……]` avec la note imprimée « N.B : * [….] indicates that a part of the text has been removed. »

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.142> **SPEAKING INTO READING / IN PAIRS** — activité **1** : « Discuss which words in the box
   can replace the underlined ones in the paragraph below. » Boîte imprimée : `emerging ; host ;
   infancy ; instantly ; intrusive ; literally ; location ; pinpoint`.
   *Clé (guide p.30)* : Identify = pinpoint ; Place = location ; Expanding = emerging ;
   Immediately = instantly ; Exactly = literally ; Babyhood = infancy ; Number = host ;
   Disturbing = intrusive.
2. <p.142> activité **2** : « Paraphrase the title of the paragraph above. Do you think that the
   fears are justified? »
3. <p.142–143> activité **3** : « Check the use of 'as'. » a) How many actions are mentioned?
   b) Are they simultaneous or does one occur before the other? c) Place the words *technology*
   and *privacy* where appropriate, on the arrows below. (schéma p.143).
4. <p.143> activités **4** et **5** : « Do YOU think that privacy diminishes due to technology
   advances? » / « Draw your own arrows to show your opinion. »
5. <p.143> **READING / IN GROUPS** — activités **1** à **7** : 1) avant lecture, répondre à
   a) What is an iPod used for? b) How long has it been in use? c) What specific features does it
   have now? d) Do you think it will remain the same in a few years' time? 2) lire le texte le plus
   vite possible et vérifier ses réponses. 3) compter le score de chaque membre du groupe sur une
   échelle de 1 à 4 (1 point par bonne réponse). 4) Who managed to answer most questions?
   5) What is your own score? 6) What about the other groups? 7) Which student has the highest
   score in the class?
6. <p.145> **Compréhension** — activités **1** à **5** : 1) le texte répond-il à la question du
   titre? pourquoi? 2) quel est le futur champ de bataille entre le concepteur de l'iPod et ses
   concurrents? 3) relever les détails montrant que ces affirmations sont fausses : a) « The iPod
   has reached full development and does not need to develop further. » b) « The iPod's designer
   has been unable to fully control the sales market. » 4) à partir du dernier paragraphe, lister
   les préoccupations des utilisateurs et les solutions possibles. 5) achèteriez-vous l'iPod de
   nouvelle génération? justifier.
   *Clé (guide p.31)* : 1. « The text does not answer the question, because no one can predict what
   the future of the iPod will be. » — 2. « The future battlefield between Apple and other
   competitors will be the 'living room', CD players, TV sets, DVD players, etc… » — 3. False
   statements : a- « the iPod ecology must grow beyond the handheld realm and inhabit the larger
   context in which we consume media » ; b- « Apple's iron-fisted control of hardware, software and
   content sales ». Le guide ouvre la leçon par une consigne au professeur : « Draw students'
   attention to the deletion in paragraph 4. […..] indicates that a part of the text has been
   removed. »
7. <p.145> **LEXICAL FOCUS** — activités **1** à **5** : 1) que signifie *omnipresent* (schéma :
   Present – everywhere / – at all times / – sometimes?). 2) quels mots du § 2 signifient à peu
   près a) a strong effect? b) surprising? c) change and modify? d) last? 3) expliquer avec ses
   propres mots, dans les §§ 2 et 4 : a) handheld digital media player b) primetime network
   programming c) semi-orphaning of the tracks… d) have grown accustomed to. 4) quels mots et
   expressions du § 3 signifient a) kingdom= b) in five years' time= c) to face= d) concerns=
   e) essential= f) strong= 5) quel mot du dernier paragraphe est employé deux fois? que
   signifie-t-il? même forme, même fonction?
   *Clé (guide p.31)* : 1. « 'omnipresent' means almost = present everywhere at all times ».
   2. a- a strong effect = impact ; b- surprising = astonishing ; c- change and modify = shift ;
   d- last = ultimate. 4. a) realm ; b) five years from now ; c) inhabit ; d) issues ; e) key ;
   f) iron-fisted. 5. « the word used twice is : sound ».
   ⚠️ Le c) de l'activité 4 demande « to face » : la clé officielle répond *inhabit* alors que le
   mot du § 3 signifiant « to face » est *confront* (voir § Incertitudes).
8. <p.146> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — « The following sentences are taken from the text. Read them and
   find out what they have in common. » Trois phrases imprimées : « It's been quite an autumn for
   the iPod. » / « The impact of the iPod on media has been astonishing. » / « Users have grown
   accustomed to hearing CD-quality and DVD surround sound on their living room systems. » Puis :
   « What tense is used in the 3 sentences? » et « Which uses does it have? Tick where appropriate. »
   Liste à cocher : 1) an action that took place in the past with no time indication ; 2) an action
   that happened in a precise time in the past ; 3) a past action with a present effect ; 4) an
   action continuing from past to present ; 5) a recent action. Ligne à compléter :
   `……..?......... + …………..?..............  ……………..?.....................`
   *Clé (guide p.31)* : « Have / has + Past Participle → Present Perfect. This tense is used for :
   – an action that happened in the past, but the time is not indicated ✓ ; – a past action with a
   present effect ✓ ; – an action continuing from past to present ✓ ; – a recent action ✓ »
   (l'item 2 « a precise time in the past » n'est donc **pas** coché).

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim, p.146)**

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> astonishing,impact,shift key,realm,confront, issue,primetime, sound(n), sound(v),
> omnipresent, troublesome,iron-fisted, ultimate, accustomed to.

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> The Present Perfect
> It's been quite an autumn for the iPod

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Express my opinion
> • Guess the meaning of unfamiliar words from the context
> • Explain words and phrases in my own words
> • Manipulate and use the Present Perfect tense
> ……?.........
> ……?.........
> ……?.........

**Lexique de la leçon** : emerging, host, infancy, instantly, intrusive, literally, location,
pinpoint ; astonishing, impact, shift, key, realm, confront, issue, primetime, sound (n. et v.),
omnipresent, troublesome, iron-fisted, ultimate, accustomed to ; handheld digital media player,
primetime network programming, semi-orphaning, have grown accustomed to.

#### Lesson 3 — INTERVIEWING THE FATHER OF PLAY STATION (p.147–150)

- **Skills blend** : SPEAKING INTO LISTENING (bandeau p.147), sous-bandeau **IN PAIRS**
- **Constructing meaning** : Listening to an interview
- **Communicating** : « Speaking about Nobel Prize winners » (cellule imprimée — voir § Incertitudes)
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : — (colonne vide ; l'encadré MY GRAMMAR p.150 travaille
  `to be dedicated / devoted / used / accustomed to + V-ing / to + something`)
- **Pronunciation** : Pronouncing *-ed* (regular past tense) ; / t ∫/ / ∫ / /z/ /dz/ /z/

**Supports** (p.147) : liste de 14 items à trier en bénéfices / inconvénients des jeux vidéo ;
paragraphe biographique à trous sur Ken Kutaragi (source implicite *Newsweek*) ; logo PlayStation.
(p.148) **document sonore** : interview de Ken Kutaragi en 2 sections (le tapescript figure dans le
guide, **guide p.50**, « The Father of PlayStation », interview de Steven Levy pour *Newsweek*,
par téléphone depuis Tokyo). (p.149) tableau d'appariement Column A / Column B ; paragraphe à trous
sur la popularité des jeux vidéo. (p.150) liste de 17 verbes réguliers à classer par prononciation
du *-ed*.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.147> **SPEAKING INTO LISTENING / IN PAIRS** — « Discuss the following. » activité **1** :
   « Are video games popular? Why? »
2. <p.147> activité **2** : « Identify the benefits and drawbacks of video games in the following
   list. » Liste imprimée : entertainment / violence / flexibility / encourage consumption of
   alcohol / concentration / smoking / interactivity / develop analytical ability / adaptability /
   addiction / develop cognitive skills / violent behaviour / negative influence / patience /
   neglect studies.
3. <p.147> activité **3** : « Do you know the most famous console? »
4. <p.147> activité **4** : paragraphe à trous (6 trous) sur Ken Kutaragi, boîte de mots
   `creator - via - perfecting - game - rare - dedicated`. Texte : « Ken Kutaragi, the head of Sony
   Computer Entertainment, says he has ------ his 'life and passion' to ------ the electronic
   gaming experience. On the eve of the Play-Station 3 launch, the ------ of all three generations
   of the hit ------ console, took time off to give a ------ interview to Newsweek ------ phone
   from Tokyo. »
   *Clé (guide p.31)* : « Information about Ken Kutaragi : dedicated – perfecting – creator – game
   – rare – via ».
5. <p.147> activités **5** et **6** : « If you could meet Ken Kutaragi, what questions would you ask
   him? » / « Use your questions to interview Ken Kutaragi after you listen to the recording. »
6. <p.148> **LISTEN to this interview with Ken Kutaragi.** — **Listen to section 1**, activité **1**
   « Correct these statements. » : a) Players will see that Play Station 3 is superior to PS2 as
   soon as it is launched. b) Play Station 3 will be released in the time frame previously
   announced by Sony. c) Play Station 3 contains the best technology.
   *Clé (guide p.31)* : « 1. a) within a year b) it will be delayed c) the latest technology ».
7. <p.148> activité **2** : « Circle the titles of Play Station games that Ken Kutaragi mentions in
   the interview. » a) Tomb Raider b) Ridge Racer c) Dark Angel d) Street Fighter e) Motor Storm
   f) Gran Turismo.
   *Clé (guide p.31)* : « Motor Storm - Ridge Racer - Gran Turismo ».
8. <p.148> activité **3** : a) cocher les titres connus ; b) souligner ceux qu'on aime ; c) barrer
   ceux qu'on n'aime pas.
9. <p.148> activité **4** : « Tick the correct alternative. The Blu-ray has (15-150-50) gigabyte
   capacity. » — *aucune clé dans le guide, aucune mention dans le tapescript* (§ Incertitudes).
10. <p.148> activité **5** : « Find out why Ken Kutaragi apologizes to the users. »
    *Clé (tapescript, guide p.50)* : le PS3 a pris du retard parce qu'il embarquait la toute
    dernière technologie (Blu-ray et autres) ; en tant que « father of the PlayStation » il présente
    ses excuses à tous les utilisateurs qui attendent la PS3 — « But everything is fixed. »
11. <p.148> **Listen to section 2** — activité **1** : « Fill in the table with the prices. »
    Tableau à 3 colonnes : *Play station 3* | *Play station 2* | *Disk drive*.
    *Clé (tapescript, guide p.50)* : PS3 = $500 et $600 ; PS2 = $299 à l'origine aux États-Unis ;
    hard-disk drive = $200 de plus (« So the total price was almost equal to PS3's initial pricing »).
12. <p.148> activité **2** : « Tick the correct alternative. PS3 launch was delayed because
    a) its price is extremely high. b) it contains the latest technologies. c) it will have a
    profound impact on every player ».
    *Clé (tapescript, guide p.50)* : b) — « because the PS3 contained the latest technology —
    Blu-ray, and other things ».
13. <p.149> activités **3** à **6** : 3) « List 2 reasons that make PS3 significant. » 4) « When does
    Ken Kutaragi feel happy? » 5) « What helps him concentrate on the development of Play Station? »
    6) « How can Play Station be used in the future? »
    *Clé partielle (tapescript, guide p.50)* : 3) l'impact sera significatif non seulement pour Sony
    mais pour les autres fabricants d'électronique qui vendent des écrans plats, et aussi pour les
    développeurs de contenus. 6) comme *home-media server* (« like a giant TV for people's movies,
    TV shows, music and personal content ») ; dans un futur proche, Sony ou des tiers sortiront un
    système de stockage en réseau de capacité multi-téraoctets. — 4) et 5) : sans réponse ni dans la
    clé ni dans le tapescript reproduit (§ Incertitudes).
14. <p.149> **LEXICAL FOCUS** — activité **1** : apparier Column A (a) via b) release c) delay
    d) giant e) envision f) initial g) maybe) et Column B (1- put off 2- imagine a future situation
    3- very large 4- launch 5- first 6- perhaps, probably 7- by means of, through 8- free).
    *Clé (guide p.32)* : a+7 / b+4 / c+1 / d+3 / e+2 / f+5 / g+6 (l'item 8- *free* est un
    distracteur).
15. <p.149> activité **2** : « Select 7 words from the box to complete the paragraph below. » Boîte
    de 8 mots : `interact - release - around - virtual - maybe - envision - via - electronic`.
    Paragraphe (7 trous) sur la popularité croissante des jeux vidéo, l'âge moyen du joueur (33
    ans), le jeu multijoueur et les communautés en ligne.
    *Clé (guide p.32)* : envision – interact – via – maybe – virtual – around – release.
16. <p.150> **PRONUNCIATION FOCUS** — activité **1** : « Say these verbs aloud. How is the final
    -ed pronounced? Put each verb under the appropriate heading. » 17 verbes : released - delayed -
    fixed - rushed - lived - adopted - needed - placed - looked - watched - begged - stopped -
    stuffed - called - envisioned - changed - formed. Tableau à 3 colonnes imprimé avec la règle :
    `/ id /` « When the verb ends with -d or -t » | `/t/` « When the verb ends with -p,-k, -s, - ch,
    sh, » | `/d/` « All the other verbs. »
17. <p.150> activité **2** : « Which verbs are stressed on the second syllable? »
18. <p.150> activité **3** : « Are the underlined sounds similar or different? » a. envi**si**on -
    gara**g**e b. fu**t**ure - ini**t**ial c. relea**s**e - u**s**er d. **g**iant - en**g**ineer.

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim, p.150)**

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> delay , envision , giant, initial , maybe , release , via

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> to be ⎧ dedicated / devoted / used / accustomed ⎫ - to doing something
>                                                 ⎭ - to something

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> - Work with a peer to perform a task
> - Pronounce past endings of regular verbs correctly and sort out verbs per sound
> - Reinvest newly acquired vocabulary in a paragraph
> - Rephrase expressions from a text in my own words
> - ……………………………?....................................
> -……………………………?....................................

**Lexique de la leçon** : delay, envision, giant, initial, maybe, release, via ; dedicated,
devoted, used to, accustomed to, perfecting, creator, rare ; interact, virtual, around, electronic ;
bénéfices/inconvénients des jeux vidéo (entertainment, interactivity, adaptability, concentration,
patience, develop analytical ability, develop cognitive skills / violence, addiction, smoking,
violent behaviour, negative influence, neglect studies, encourage consumption of alcohol).

#### Lesson 4 — PRIZE WINNERS (p.151–155)

- **Skills blend** : READING INTO WRITING (bandeau p.151)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading notes about prize winners and inventors
- **Communicating** : Writing — Imitating a model ; Making notes about famous inventors using web
  resources
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : — (colonne vide ; l'encadré MY GRAMMAR p.155 travaille
  **The passive**)
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.151) : encadré de notes « Great men and women are quite creative in several
fields… » ; paragraphe à trous sur le **WIPO Gold Medal Award** (10 trous, sur p.151–152).
(p.152) suite du paragraphe ; paragraphe à trous « **Alfred Nobel (1833 - 1896) — The Man Behind
the Nobel Prize** » (6 trous). (p.153) texte informatif sur le Nobel avec 15 mots entre parenthèses
à dériver. (p.154) liste « **Nobel Prize Winners** » : 6 lauréats avec année et citation officielle
du jury. (p.155) déclaration de **Charles Lamb**, auteur des *Shakespeare's Tales*, sur Shakespeare
(3 lignes, modèle à imiter).

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.151> **READING INTO WRITING — A. READ, REFLECT, REACT, SHARE** — activité **1** : lire les
   notes sur les gens créatifs puis a) penser à un nom spécialisé dans chaque domaine de créativité ;
   b) préparer une liste de noms avec les domaines correspondants ; c) faire le tour de la classe
   pour voir quels noms et domaines les camarades ont trouvés ; d) ajouter les noms jugés importants ;
   e) partager sa liste ; f) penser à une liste de groupe ; g) voir comment utiliser au mieux la liste.
   Notes imprimées : « Great men and women are quite creative in several fields : literature,
   science, technology, inventions and so on… The best novel, the best invention and the best
   research deserve prizes. Worldwide famous prizes, are awarded to the most creative scientist,
   inventor, researcher, novelist or playwright. »
2. <p.151–152> activité **2** : « Insert the words in the box where appropriate to get a coherent
   paragraph. » Boîte (10 mots) : `tribute - share - inventive - fields - commitment - ingredient -
   those - confidence - recognition - distinctions`. Texte sur le **WIPO Gold Medal Award scheme**
   (1979), les 747 inventeurs de 93 pays récompensés jusqu'en décembre 2002, et les femmes
   inventrices. Le manuel développe le sigle en « (Women Invent Property Organization), WIPO »
   [sic — *World Intellectual Property Organization*].
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : « recognition – distinctions – inventive – those – inventing – fields –
   share – confidence – ingredients – tributr [sic] – commitment. » (voir § Incertitudes : 11 items
   pour 10 trous, ordre non aligné sur les trous).
3. <p.152> activité **3** : « Once you complete the paragraph, find synonyms of the following
   words. » a) reward = praise = b) award = prize = c) paying no attention = d) features =
   e) being sure about one's abilities = f) gift =
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : a. award ; b. medal = distinction ; c. regardless ; d. characteristics ;
   e. confidence ; f. talent.
4. <p.152> activité **4** : 8 questions — a) Which is your favourite field of creativity? b) Do you
   know any creative people around you? c) Have you heard about international bodies that reward
   creative people? d) Can you name some of those organisations? e) What do you know about the
   Nobel Prize? f) Who is it named after? g) Who won the Prize in 2006? h) What field was in focus?
5. <p.152> activité **5** : « Use the words in the box to complete the paragraph below. » Boîte :
   `prize - achievements - establishment - peace - inventor - leaving`. Paragraphe sur Alfred Nobel
   (1833–1896) et le testament de 1895.
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : achievements - peace – prize – inventor – leaving - establishment.
6. <p.153> activité **6** : « Read the information about the Nobel Prize and a) supply the correct
   form of the bracketed words b) identify the suffixes used to form the adjectives c) rephrase this
   expression in your own words : "prize-awarding institution" ». Mots entre parenthèses, dans
   l'ordre du texte : (prestige), (signify), (science), (develop), (invite), (science), (come),
   (represent), (special), (evaluate), (select), (recommend), (respect), (take), (die).
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : prestigious – significant – scientific – development – invitations –
   scientists – coming – represented – specially – evaluates – selection – recommendations –
   respectful [sic, lire *respective*] – taken – death.
7. <p.153–154> activité **7** : « Read the information about the Nobel Prize winners and a) answer
   the questions. » — « Who won the Nobel Prize in Literature? » / « Who was awarded the Nobel Prize
   in Chemistry? » / « Who was the Nobel Prize in Technology awarded to? » puis b) « say what you
   notice about the 3 questions. Is the structure the same? Why? Why not? » (contraste actif /
   passif — c'est le point de langue de la leçon).
   **Liste imprimée p.154 — Nobel Prize Winners** : *Physics* MARIE CURIE - France, 1903, « in
   recognition of the extraordinary services she has rendered by her research on the radiation
   phenomena discovered by Professor Henri Becquerel » ; *Chemistry* DOROTHY CROWFOOT HODGKIN -
   Great Britain, 1964, « for her determinations by X-ray techniques of the structures of important
   biochemical substances » ; *Physiology or Medicine* ANDREW Z. FIRE and CRAIG C. MELLO - USA,
   2006, « for discovering a mechanism for controlling the flow of genetic information » ;
   *Literature* ORHAN PAMUK - Turkey, 2006, « who in the quest for the melancholic soul of his
   native city has discovered new symbols for the clash and interlacing of cultures » ; *Peace*
   MUHAMMAD YUNUS and GRAMEEN BANK - Bangladesh, 2006, « for developing micro-credit as an
   instrument in the struggle against poverty » ; *Prize in Economics* EDMUND S. PHELPS - USA, 2006,
   « for changing our perceptions of the tradeoffs between economic objectives ».
8. <p.153> activités **8** à **12** : 8) faire des phrases sur les lauréats à partir des questions de
   7.a. 9) « Who, among the Prize winners listed on the next page is your favourite? Justify your
   choice. » 10) revenir à la liste de noms constituée en 1.d;e;f;g et vérifier si des noms ou des
   domaines figurent parmi les lauréats. 11) « Would you like to win a prize for your creativity? »
   12) « What impact would it have on you? »
9. <p.154> activité **c)** (en pied de la liste des lauréats) : « Refer to the definition of
   'brainchild' in the Introductory Unit and say whose brainchild each invention is. »
10. <p.155> **DEVELOP YOUR WRITING SKILL — Imitate the model** — activité **1** : lire la déclaration
    de Charles Lamb, auteur des *Shakespeare's Tales* : « Shakespeare was not just a great writer but
    the greatest writer who had ever lived, a figure who in many ways was greater than ordinary
    people and whose knowledge and understanding of human nature was wide and deep ».
11. <p.155> activité **2** : a) penser à un(e) grand(e) écrivain, inventeur, scientifique ou
    chercheur et produire une déclaration louant ses qualités et son œuvre, en réutilisant les mots
    surlignés et la structure de Charles Lamb ; amorce imposée :
    `---------------------(name of person) was not just a -----------------but the-------------------`
    b) lire sa déclaration à toute la classe.
12. <p.155> **WEBQUEST** : accéder au site du NOBEL PRIZE, trouver les différents lauréats du
    XXI<sup>e</sup> siècle, faire une liste d'inventeurs qui ont mérité le prix entre 2001 et 2006,
    chercher des détails sur leurs inventions et les insérer dans le **PORTFOLIO**.
    *Clé (guide p.32)* : « Get students to access the Nobel Prize website and identify the winners in
    the 21st century in various fields. They may share their findings with the rest of the class.
    Finding information, sorting it out and organising notes is an essential skill to develop in
    learners at this level. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim, p.155)**

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> creativity, founder, entrepreneur, honouring, a will.

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> The passive
> The Nobel Prize is awarded to inventors
> Inventors are awarded the Nobel Prize
> The inventors win the Nobel Prize

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Collect information about Alfred
> • Nobel
> • Compare Nobel Prize winners
> • Talk about prize-awarding organisations
> • Imitate a model in writing
> • Share,reflect,react,compare
> • Search information on the web
> …….?..............................
> …….?..............................

**Lexique de la leçon** : creativity, founder, entrepreneur, honouring, a will ; tribute, share,
inventive, fields, commitment, ingredient, confidence, recognition, distinction(s), award, medal,
regardless, characteristics, talent ; prestigious, significant, scientific, development, invitation,
scientist, represented, specially, evaluate, selection, recommendation, respective, laureate,
nominate, brainchild ; achievements, peace, prize, inventor, establishment.

#### Lesson 5 — WOMEN CHOOSE TO OPT OUT (p.156–160)

- **Skills blend** : READING INTO WRITING (bandeau p.156)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading an argumentative article
- **Communicating** : Writing — Summarizing an article
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Nouns ending in *ship* ; The Subjunctive
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.156–157) : article argumentatif découpé en 5 paragraphes mélangés, attribution
imprimée *By Susan West Expert Author — www.womenbiznews.com*. Le paragraphe 1 (thèse principale)
est **donné comme SAMPLE ANSWER** ; les 4 autres sont à numéroter et à étiqueter (3 arguments +
1 conclusion). Sources citées dans le texte : Cheskin, Center for Women's Business Research,
Catalyst. (p.158) tableau d'appariement lexical. (p.159) 7 étapes du résumé.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.156–157> activité **1** : « The following scrambled paragraphs make up an argumentative
   article. Reorder them so as to get a main thesis, 3 supporting arguments and a conclusion.
   Number each paragraph and label it. *See the sample answer provided.* » Paragraphes imprimés
   (dans l'ordre du manuel) : « Women used to be willing to devote their time… » / « Expanding
   opportunities for women in leadership will require corporate change… » / « Experts say corporate
   America isn't doing enough… » / [*SAMPLE ANSWER* — Paragraph N° 1 : Main thesis] « Increasingly,
   women managers are choosing to "opt out"… » / « Fifty-one percent of women business owners with
   prior private-sector experience… ».
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : « Paragraph N°2 — Supporting argument : *Women used to be willing…* ;
   Paragraph N°3 — Supporting argument : *Fifty-one percent of women…* ; Paragraph N°4 — Supporting
   argument : *Experts say corporate America…* ; Paragraph N°4 — Conclusion : *Expanding
   opportunities for women…* » (le guide numérote deux fois « N°4 » ; la conclusion est en réalité
   le paragraphe 5 — voir § Incertitudes).
2. <p.157> activité **2** : « What type of article is this? a) narrative b) descriptive
   c) argumentative ».
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : c.
3. <p.157> activité **3** : « Explain the title of the article. »
4. <p.157> activité **4** : « Read the text and complete the table » — tableau à 3 lignes :
   *Characters* / *Decision* / *Cause*.
5. <p.157> activité **5** : « Complete the sentence. Women would have brought a great potential to
   their companies if ………………. »
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : « Women would have brought a great potential to their companies if they had
   been given the opportunity » (3<sup>e</sup> conditionnel).
6. <p.157> activités **6** et **7** : « What can companies do to keep women from opting out? » /
   « Do you think that high potential persons should leave a company and start their own business?
   Support your opinion with good arguments. »
7. <p.158> **LEXICAL FOCUS** — activité **1** : apparier 1. paths / 2. at the expense of /
   3. recognition / 4. cite / 5. hold s.o. accountable for sthg / 6. significant / 7. valued
   à a) consider s/he is responsible b) considered important c) ways of achieving something
   d) public praise and reward e) with damage to f) mention something as a reason g) important.
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : 1-c, 2-e, 3-d, 4-f, 5-a, 6-b, 7-g.
   ⚠️ Le guide imprime « 6-b 7-g » : *significant* → b) *considered important* et *valued* →
   g) *important*.
8. <p.158> activité **2** : « "catch phrase" means a) a popular phrase b) a meaningful sentence
   c) an important saying ».
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : a.
9. <p.158> activité **3** : « Paraphrase the following expressions. » — prior private-sector
   experience / non-profit research / restrictive glass-ceiling issues / women business owners /
   twice the rate of men.
   *Clé (guide p.32)* : « – previous experience in the private sector ; – research without the aim
   of making profit ; – issues about the imaginary barrier that stops women from getting the best
   jobs in a company ; – women owning a business ; – two times as many as men ».
10. <p.158> activité **4** : « Pick out 2 words spelt the American way. »
    *Clé (guide p.32)* : favor - Center.
11. <p.158> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : à partir de « It is essential that a company
    identify potential women managers. » a) « Focus on the form of the verb. What do you notice? »
    b) « Complete : It is essential that + subject + verb in the …………… » c) « Choose the correct
    alternatives. This tense (……………) is used in (formal / informal), (personal / impersonal)
    English. »
    *Clé (guide p.33)* : b) « It is essential that + subject + verb in the Subjunctive » ;
    c) « The Subjunctive is used in formal, impersonal English. »
12. <p.159> activité **1.d)** : « Use the right form of the bracketed verbs. » • It is advisable
    that a scientist (to be) … patient. • It is important that he (to have) … a deep curiosity.
    • We are suggesting that he (to reconsider) … our proposals in his research. • We insist that
    he (to respect) … safety measures when carrying out his experiments.
    *Clé (guide p.33)* : be, have, reconsider, respect.
13. <p.159> activité **2** : a) « Focus on the word *leadership*. What do you notice about this
    word? How is it formed? » `………….?.............. + ……..?............  …………?....................`
    b) « What do you call – the fact of owning something? – the state of being a citizen? – being a
    member of a group? – the status of a university professor? – relation between people? – a
    partner in a project or a business? »
    *Clé (guide p.33)* : ownership - citizenship - membership - professorship - relationship -
    partnership. N.B. du guide : « being partners or having a partner in a project = partnership ».
14. <p.159> **DEVELOP YOUR WRITING SKILL** — les 7 étapes imprimées du résumé : 1. Sum up each
    paragraph in ONE sentence. 2. Focus on only one main point or idea. 3. Make sure no key idea has
    been ignored. 4. Link your sentences with appropriate connectors. 5. Proofread your summary
    paying attention to grammar, vocabulary, punctuation and spelling. 6. Hand in your summary to
    your teacher for evaluation. 7. Once you get it back, further improve it before you insert it in
    your PORTFOLIO.
    *Clé (guide p.33)* : « Summary — The 5 steps (1 to 5) should be strictly respected. Step 7 is
    also important for a further editing and refining of the summary. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim, p.160)**

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> at the expense of , recognition, cite, hold s.o. accountable for, Paths, significant, valued

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> - The subjunctive
> - It is important that he come on time
> - Nouns ending in -ship
>   noun+ship
>   Leader + ship
>   Relation+ ship

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Reorder scrambled paragraphs of a text
> • Match components of an argumentative text with paragraphs
> • Paraphrase grammatical and lexical phrases
> • Use main ideas to sum up an argumentative article
> • Make adjustments,proofread and finalize a summary
> …………..? …………………….
> Anything else?

**Lexique de la leçon** : at the expense of, recognition, cite, hold s.o. accountable for, paths,
significant, valued ; opt out, catch phrase, glass ceiling, non-profit, prior, entrepreneurship,
high-visibility assignment, succession planning, high-potential ; noms en *-ship* (leadership,
ownership, citizenship, membership, professorship, relationship, partnership) ; orthographes
américaines *favor*, *center*.

#### Lesson 6 — THE BRAIN DRAIN (p.161–167)

- **Skills blend** : READING INTO WRITING (bandeau p.161)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading three short texts ; Comparing texts
- **Communicating** : Writing a formal letter
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : `So that` (purpose) ; `So …. that` (result)
- **Pronunciation** : Syllable stress — Classifying words ; Identifying number of syllables

**Supports** (p.161) : deux bulles à relier par des flèches (*One's country* / *Foreign countries*) ;
spidergram vierge « Why people emigrate » ; notes à développer en définition. (p.162) **TEXT A**
« How Extensive Is the Brain Drain? » — *International Monetary Fund - June 1999*, ≈ 10 lignes ;
flow chart à compléter (« Educated people emigrate » → conséquences). (p.163) **TEXT B**
« International Migration: Brain Drain or Brain Gain? » — *The World Bank Group, 31 October 2005*,
≈ 10 lignes ; tableau *Developed countries* / *Developing countries*. (p.164) **TEXT C** « Reverse
Brain Drain: A Loss or A Gain? » — *www.homecomingrevolution.com*, ≈ 10 lignes, contenant **une
phrase intruse** à barrer. (p.165) boîte de 9 mots/expressions à définir ; 3 idiomes. (p.166) liste
de 20 mots à classer par syllabe accentuée.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.161> **READING INTO WRITING** — activité **1** : « Show the difference between : migrate -
   emigrate - immigrate on the two bubbles below using arrows in the correct direction(s) »
   (bulles *One's country* / *Foreign countries*).
2. <p.161> activité **2** : « Complete the spidergram with the causes that make people emigrate. »
   (schéma central « Why people emigrate »).
3. <p.161> activité **3** : a) « Develop the notes into a definition. Do not change the order of the
   words. Add articles, prepositions, relative pronouns and linkers. Put the verbs in the correct
   tense or form. » Notes imprimées : `The Brain Drain / movement / highly / skill / qualify /
   people / developing / nations / developed / countries / earn / more money.`
   d) [sic — devrait être b)] « Go to the Introductory Unit dictionary page to check the meaning of
   'brain drain'. »
   *Clé (guide p.33)* : « The brain drain is the movement of highly skilled and qualified people
   from developing countries to developed countries where they can earn more money. »
4. <p.162> **A. Read Text A and** activité **1** : « fill in the flow chart with the consequences of
   emigration » (départ imprimé : *Educated people emigrate*).
5. <p.162> activité **2** : « What solution does the writer suggest to solve the problem of brain
   drain? »
6. <p.162> activité **3** : « These are synonyms of 3 words from the text. Identify the 3 words. »
   a) effect, result, consequence, outcome = b) useless, vain, inefficient, fruitless, worthless =
   c) lack, absence, scarcity, insufficiency =
   *Clé (guide p.33)* : a- implication ; b- futile ; c- shortage.
7. <p.162> activité **4** : « What do you think a 'physician' means? a) a physics teacher b) a
   doctor c) a physicist d) an inventor ».
   *Clé (guide p.33)* : b) a doctor.
8. <p.162> activité **5** : « Express the last sentence in the text differently. »
9. <p.163> **B. TEXT B** — activité **1** (avant lecture) : « What do you think Text B is about?
   Tick the possible answers. » a) the different facets of human migration b) migration can't be
   avoided c) migration is dangerous d) the causes and effects of human migration.
   *Clé (guide p.33)* : « Tick the possible answers : a-c-d ».
10. <p.163> activités **2** et **3** : « Explain the title in your own words. » / « Do you think
    human migration is inevitable? »
11. <p.163> après lecture, activité **1** : « complete the table with the benefits of human
    migration to » — colonnes *Developed countries* / *Developing countries*.
12. <p.163> activité **2** : « say whether the World Bank worries about the impact of brain drain on
    developing nations. Do you find their arguments convincing? »
13. <p.164> **C. TEXT C** — activité **1** : a) « find an irrelevant sentence and cross it out. »
    b) « explain what a "reverse brain drain" is. »
    *Clé (guide p.33)* : « 1. a- *They thrive as they enjoy their full rights in their homeland*
    (odd sentence to cross out) ».
14. <p.164> activité **2** : a) « What, in your opinion, are the reasons that make Africans come
    home? » b) « How can reverse brain drain bring an economic recovery? » c) « What do you think of
    the immigrants' new attitude? »
15. <p.164> **D. Comparing texts** — activités **1** à **4** : 1) « Which is your favourite text?
    A, B or C? » 2) « What are the reasons for your choice? » 3) « What words and expressions did you
    appreciate most? » 4) « Can the 3 short texts make up a coherent long one? Why? Why not? »
16. <p.164> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : « What does the sentence : "so that developing
    nations are not unnecessarily hurt" express? a) cause b) purpose c) result ».
17. <p.165> activité **2** : « Rephrase the sentences in bold type using *so that* or *so…… that*. »
    Paragraphe imprimé : « When I was young, I was eager to become a scientist. I carried out
    experiments in our garage. I studied hard because I wanted my parents to let me choose my career
    path. I wanted to invent a wonderful new product which would make the world a better place.
    Unfortunately, I was very bad at technical matters. I often had misadventures. Now I realize I
    am clumsy. I feel I can never become an inventor. To my great embarrassment, it is always a
    child of six who helps me out of my difficulties. »
    *Clé (guide p.33)* : « When I was young, I was **so** eager to become a scientist **that** I
    carried out experiments in our garage. I studied hard **so that** my parents let me choose my
    career path. …………., I was **so** bad at technical matters **that** I often had misadventures.
    Now I realize I am **so** clumsy **that** I feel I can never become an inventor. »
18. <p.165> **LEXICAL FOCUS** — activité **1** : travail à deux (Student A choisit un mot, Student B
    donne la définition). Boîte : `trend / gather pace / expertise / key positions / contentious /
    in search of / incentive / opportunity / supply`. Définitions 1 à 9, les deux premières données
    en exemple : 1. likely to cause disagreement between people = **contentious** ; 2. a reason that
    encourages you to do something = **incentive** ; 3. occasion= 4. a general direction in which a
    situation is changing= 5. provide= 6. increase in number= 7. high jobs= 8. looking for=
    9. skill in a particular subject=
    *Clé (guide p.33)* : 3. opportunity ; 4. trend ; 5. supply ; 6. gather pace ; 7. key positions ;
    8. in search of ; 9. expertise.
19. <p.165–166> activité **2** : « Tick the alternatives that best explain the following idioms. »
    – *Play the cards right* : a) deal successfully with a particular situation b) play the cards
    well c) play the game right. – *Swim against the tide* : a) swim in a dangerous sea b) speak in
    a difficult situation c) oppose the attitudes or opinions that most other people have.
    – *Sow the seeds of …* : a) start the process that leads to a particular result b) plant grains
    c) start coming back.
    *Clé (guide p.33)* : « Idioms : meaning — a / c / a ».
20. <p.166> **PRONUNCIATION** — activité **1** : « Classify these words (taken from Texts A, B and C)
    according to the stressed syllables they contain. » Liste : political, expertise, revival,
    contribute, demographic, declining, developing, expatriates, especially, inevitable, economic,
    international, contentious, physicians, professionals, shortages, educational, phenomenon,
    implication, investment. Sous-questions : mots accentués sur la 1<sup>re</sup> syllabe? sur la
    2<sup>e</sup>? sur la 3<sup>e</sup>?
21. <p.166> activités **2** et **3** : « How many syllables does each word contain? Use a slash to
    indicate each syllable. Example : po/li/ti/cal (4 syllables) » / « How many four-syllable words
    did you get? »
22. <p.166> **DEVELOP YOUR WRITING SKILL — Writing a formal letter.** « According to the World Bank
    Group, brain drain is beneficial to developing countries. What do you think? Write a letter to
    The World Bank Group in which you state your opinion about the issue giving sound arguments that
    support your viewpoint. Mind the layout of the letter and the degree of formality of the
    language you will use. »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim, p.167)**

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> contentious , expertise , gather pace, incentive key positions, in search of, opportunity,
> trends, shortage , supply

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> So that (purpose)
> So……that(result)

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Read and understand 3 different texts
> • Appreciate linguistic aspects of various texts
> • Match synonyms
> • Transfer information onto a flow chart
> • Interpret idioms
> • Express cause,purpose and result
> • Divide words ino syllables and identify stress place
> • Write a formal letter
> • ……….?.......................
> • ……….?.......................

**Lexique de la leçon** : contentious, expertise, gather pace, incentive, key positions, in search
of, opportunity, trend(s), shortage, supply ; migrate / emigrate / immigrate, brain drain, brain
gain, reverse brain drain, expatriates, implication, futile, physician, inevitable, thrive,
persecution, revival ; idiomes *play the cards right*, *swim against the tide*, *sow the seeds of*.

#### Lesson 7 — SCIENTISTS' ACHIEVEMENTS (p.168–173)

- **Skills blend** : READING INTO SPEAKING (bandeau p.168), sous-bandeau **IN GROUPS**, puis
  bandeau **READING**
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading 2 texts
- **Communicating** : Speaking — Taking part in a video conference
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Restrictive / Non-restrictive clauses
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.168) : boîte de 15 qualités d'inventeur ; photos de laboratoire et de microscope ;
tableau *Name / Country of origin / Invention / Benefits of the invention*. (p.169) reportage CNN
sur **Ahmed H. Zewail** (prix Nobel de chimie, femtochimie), attribution *www.cnn.com*, ≈ 20 lignes,
avec le sous-titre imprimé « 'No chemical reactions take place faster' ». (p.169–170) dépêche sur
**James Hillier**, coinventeur du microscope électronique, attribution *January 18, 2007 News Story
from The Trenton Times*, ≈ 15 lignes. (p.172) encadré **DID YOU KNOW …?** (6 faits d'histoire des
inventions).

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.168> **READING INTO SPEAKING / IN GROUPS** — activité **1** : « Circle the words which
   describe the qualities of an inventor. » Liste : dedication - knowledge - love - curiosity -
   nature - imagination - chance - commitment - research - experience - inspiration - experiment -
   intensive work - science - motivation.
2. <p.168> activité **2** : « What kind of career would you like to have? Check with your group
   members which career they would like to have? Why? What about studying abroad and conducting
   research in a renowned international laboratory? Do you know any famous scientists who went
   abroad to conduct research and succeeded in their careers? Make a list which you will compare to
   other classmates' lists. »
3. <p.168–169> **READING — A.1** : « Read this CNN (Cable News Network) news report about a famous
   inventor and complete the table. » Tableau : *Name* | *Country of origin* | *Invention* |
   *Benefits of the invention*. (attendu d'après le texte : Ahmed H. Zewail ; Egyptian-American /
   Égypte + USA ; la femtochimie — technique laser ultra-rapide observant le mouvement des atomes
   pendant une réaction chimique ; applications : conception de composants électroniques
   moléculaires, étude des mécanismes du vivant, production des médicaments de demain.)
4. <p.169> **A.2** : « Explain the invention in your own words. »
5. <p.169–170> **B.** « Read this news story about James Hillier, inventor of the electron
   microscope and answer the questions. » activités **1** à **8** : 1) Avec qui James Hillier a-t-il
   partagé le mérite de l'invention du microscope électronique? 2) Quelle différence entre
   microscope optique et microscope électronique? 3) Le microscope électronique était-il sa seule
   invention? justifier. 4) Quelles sont ses autres contributions à la recherche? 5) Est-il mort
   dans sa ville natale? 6) Quel est le montant de la dotation qu'il a donnée en bourses d'études?
   7) Lire le chiffre à voix haute pour montrer qu'on sait le prononcer. 8) Que remarque-t-on sur la
   place du nom de la monnaie? avant ou après le montant?
   (éléments du texte : Albert Prebus, sous la direction du Professor Eli Burton, University of
   Toronto, 1938 ; grossissement 7 000 fois, plus de trois fois celui des microscopes optiques ;
   41 brevets ; don de **$500,000 US** — noter la postposition du nom de monnaie — à la James
   Hillier Foundation, 10 bourses scientifiques ; Order of Canada 1997 ; « one of the scientific
   wonders of the modern world ».)
6. <p.170> **C.** « Read both texts again and answer these questions. » 1) Quelle récompense chacun
   des 2 inventeurs a-t-il reçue? 2) Laquelle a le plus de valeur? 3) Chacune des 2 inventions
   doit-elle être considérée comme une des merveilles scientifiques du monde moderne? justifier.
   4) De quelles autres merveilles scientifiques avez-vous entendu parler?
7. <p.170> **LEXICAL FOCUS** — activité **1** : « Paraphrase the following expressions. » a) In the
   late 1980s b) We have reached the end of the road b) [sic] Have the credit for doing something.
   *Clé (guide p.33)* : a- « In the late 1980s = near the end of » ; b- « We have reached the end of
   the road = reach the point at which sth can no longer be the same » ; c- « Have the credit for =
   praise because you are responsible for sth good that has happened. »
8. <p.171> activité **2** : appariement à deux (Student A choisit un mot en colonne A, Student B
   donne la définition). Mots : 1. occur / 2. enable / 3. magnify / 4. actual / 2. [sic] patent.
   Définitions : a) real, exact b) an official right to be the only person to use or sell a product
   or an invention c) happen, take place d) make it possible for someone to do something e) make
   something look bigger than it really is.
   *Clé (guide p.33)* : 1-c, 2-d, 3-e, 4-a, 5-b.
9. <p.171> activité **3** : « Fill in the blanks with the words in the box. » Boîte (7 mots pour
   6 trous) : `enable - explore - creation - inventing - original - occur - minds`. Paragraphe sur
   l'imagination, l'inspiration et la dévotion nécessaires à toute création.
   *Clé (guide p.33)* : explore - inventing - occur - minds - creation - enable (*original* est le
   distracteur).
10. <p.171> **GRAMMAR FOCUS** — activité **1** : à partir de « James Hillier, **who shared the credit
    for inventing the electron microscope**, died Monday at age 91. » — « What do we call the
    underlined part? Is it essential to define the noun before it? What information does it add to
    the noun? »
11. <p.171> activité **2** : comparer avec « The man **who invented the telephone** was a teacher. »
    — « Is the underlined part essential to a clear understanding of the noun before it? What do you
    notice about the punctuation? »
12. <p.172> activité **3** (constat imprimé) : « The first sentence contains a non-restrictive
    (non-defining) clause whereas the second contains a restrictive (defining) clause. »
13. <p.172> activité **4** : « Now do this exercise. Check which clauses are restrictive and which
    are not. » a) the exciting game, which we had long been waiting for, began at 2pm. b) The happy
    inventor, who was thrilled with his success and prize, celebrated the event with other
    prize-winners. c) The medicine which was invented by Fleming saved many lives. d) Najib Mahfoudh
    who was awarded the Nobel Prize for Literature died in 2006. e) The man whom I met at the
    airport is a famous scientist. f) Ken Kutaragi, who invented Play Station, is Japanese.
    *Clé (guide p.34)* : « Restrictive and non restrictive clauses : see Grammar Reference at the end
    of the book. Get students to identify the difference between both types of clauses. » (le guide
    ne donne pas le détail item par item).
14. <p.172> **SPEAKING / IN PAIRS** : « You have the opportunity to take part in a videoconference
    with Dr Ahmed Zewail. Prepare a set of questions related to the doctor's scientific career, his
    feelings when he was awarded the Nobel Prize and what he thinks about young people who emigrate
    to a developed country in order to conduct research. »
15. <p.172> **WEBQUEST** : « Read the information in the box and choose a name or an invention to
    conduct a webquest which you will present to your class in the form of a poster. » Encadré
    **DID YOU KNOW …?** (verbatim) : 1. An English doctor, Sir Thomas Browny [sic], invented the word
    'electricity' in 1646. 2. In 1841, the English scientist Michael Faraday invented the first
    electric motor. 3. The Fahrenheit temperature scale is named after Gabriel Fahrenheit, a
    physicist, interested in heat, light and energy. 4. the Celsius scale was divided [sic] by a
    Swedish scientist, Anders Celsius, in 1742. 5. The first successful fountain pen was invented in
    1884 by the American Lewis Edson Waterman. 6. Blaise Pascal, a French mathematician, invented a
    mechanical calculator in 1642.

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim, p.173)**

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> actual , enable , have the credit for doing sth , magnify , occur , patent , the late (1980s) ,
> reach the end of the road.

> MY GRAMMAR
>
> Non-restrictive Clauses
> Peter, who has never driven quickly, had an accident.
>
> Restrictive Clauses
> The boy who dropped out of school regretted it.

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Identify the qualities of a scientist
> • Get to know 2 famous scientists and their inventions
> • Search information about inventors and inventions and present it on a poster
> • . ……….?.......................
> • . ……….?.......................

**Lexique de la leçon** : actual, enable, have the credit for doing sth, magnify, occur, patent,
the late (1980s), reach the end of the road ; dedication, knowledge, curiosity, imagination,
commitment, inspiration, motivation, intensive work ; femtochemistry, femtosecond, molecule,
chemical reaction, laser flashes, endowment, scholarship, magnification, metallurgy.

#### Lesson 8 — 'THE DAFFODILS by WILLIAM WORSDWORTH [sic] (p.174–179)

- **Skills blend** : — (aucun bandeau de blend imprimé p.174 ; la page ouvre directement sur la
  partie **A**)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading a poem ; Identifying Figurative Language
- **Communicating** : Writing — Summarising a poem
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : —
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.174–175) : le poème **« The Daffodils » de William Wordsworth** — 4 strophes de
6 vers (24 vers), imprimé avec une colonne latérale **Rhymes** donnant le schéma
*a b a b c c / d e d e f f / g h g h i i / j k j k l l*, accompagné d'illustrations florales.
⚠️ **Aucun vers n'est reproduit ici** (droits + R-2) : sujet — le poète, errant seul, découvre une
foule de jonquilles au bord d'un lac ; leur danse le rejoint plus tard en souvenir, dans la
solitude, et le remplit de joie. (p.174) trois définitions encadrées de termes de poésie.
(p.176) page de dictionnaire *wander* / *wonder* (entrées complètes). (p.177) deux entrées de
dictionnaire de type thésaurus, *host* (2 entrées) et *lie* (verbe, 5 sens + LIE IN / LIE LOW) ;
résumé du poème à trous. (p.178) encadré définissant le **figurative language** (metaphor, simile,
hyperbole, personification) avec la citation d'Aristote (*Rhetoric*) et l'exemple de Burns
« A Red, Red Rose ».

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.174> **A. Are you familiar with poetry-related terms?** « Which is which? Label the three
   definitions using these words : alliteration, stanza, rhyme. » Les trois définitions imprimées
   (verbatim) : « It is a group of lines of verse that make the basic unit of poetry. » / « It is the
   repeating of initial sounds as in, 'Tyger! Tyger! Burning bright!'. This often creates emphasis
   and makes words memorable. » / « It is the repeating of end of word sounds either within a line
   or at the end of two lines. »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : groupe de vers = **stanza** ; répétition des sons initiaux = **alliteration** ;
   répétition des sons de fin de mot (rime interne ou finale) = **rhyme**.
2. <p.174> **B. Read the poem and answer the questions below** — activité **1** : « Guess from the
   title the first stanza and the pictures what the poem is about. »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : « The daffodils are flowers. So the poem may be about nature. »
3. <p.174> activité **2** : « Read the whole poem and find out the general tone conveyed by the poet.
   Identify the words and phrases that illustrate the tone. »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : « The tone is that of happiness ».
4. <p.176> activité **3** : « What is the poet's relationship to nature? How does he feel about the
   natural world? »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : « The relationship of the poet with nature is that of unity, fusion and
   engulfment. He and the natural world become one. The poet and nature have 'fused'. He considers
   it as bliss… »
5. <p.176> activité **4** : « Which lines or phrases express a sense of timelessness in the poet's
   journey? »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : « Stanza 2 » (les étoiles qui brillent, la Voie lactée, la ligne sans fin…).
6. <p.176> activité **5** : « check the use of the word 'lonely'. Is it positive or negative? »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : « 'lonely' in the poem is rather positive. »
7. <p.176> activité **6** : « What proves that the poet gets pleasure from remembering the scene of
   the daffodils he saw? »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : le vers final où le cœur du poète se remplit de plaisir → « It is a happy
   experience ».
8. <p.176> activité **7** : « The final stanza of this poem simultaneously embodies images that
   express paradoxical feelings. What are they? »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : « On the one hand he is lonely 'on his couch' (feeling of emptiness and
   solitude) ; on the other hand, while being alone, his heart is filled with happiness. → Solitude
   becomes a bliss. »
9. <p.176> activité **8** : « Focus on the tenses used in the first and last stanzas. Why are these
   tenses used? »
   *Clé (guide p.34)* : « Simple past and present tenses. This implies the timelessness mentioned
   earlier. Put very simply, the poet is remembering a scene that happened in the past. It's like
   the recurrent flashbacks. In the beginning, the poet uses the past (he is narrating or recounting
   this experience). In the end, the present tense is used (he still remembers vividly the scene of
   the dancing daffodils). »
10. <p.176> activité **9** : a) « Pick out the words related to happiness. » b) « Sort them out into
    verbs, nouns and adjectives. »
    *Clé (guide p.34)* : fluttering, dancing, glee, gay, pleasure, bliss.
11. <p.176> activité **10** : « Focus on stanza 1, line 2 and stanza 4 line 1. identify 2 words in
    which some letters are missing. »
    *Clé (guide p.34)* : « O'er → over ; oft → often ».
12. <p.176> activité **11** : « Focus on stanza 1, line 1 and express it differently. Focus on the
    verb. This dictionary page will help you. » (page de dictionnaire *wander* / *wonder* imprimée :
    *wander* = marcher lentement sans but précis ; s'écarter du lieu où l'on devrait être ;
    (esprit) vagabonder — SYN : STRAY. *wonder* = se demander ; formule polie de question ; être
    très surpris.)
13. <p.177> activité **12** : a) « Which word order is used instead of Subject + Verb + Object in
    stanza 4 line 5? Why? » b) « Explain this phrase which appears in stanza 3 line 3 : *A poet could
    not but be gay*. »
    *Clé (guide p.34)* : « 12. a) the last stanza (le vers où le cœur se remplit de plaisir) —
    b) A poet cannot help (prevent himself from) feeling happy. »
    ⚠️ La clé du guide répond au a) par une référence de strophe, pas par la nomination de
    l'inversion attendue (voir § Incertitudes).
14. <p.177> **C. Dictionary Skills** : « Read the following dictionary entries. Decide which meaning
    corresponds to the one conveyed in the poem. The words are from stanzas 1 and 4. » Entrées
    imprimées : **host** (1. proprietor, proprietress, landlord, landlady, innkeeper, hotel-keeper,
    hotelier ; 2. party-giver, entertainer ; 3. presenter, master of ceremonies ; Antonyms : GUEST —
    puis 2<sup>e</sup> entrée : multitude, crowd, throng, mob, army, herd, pack, flock, swarm, troop,
    band, mass, assemblage, assembly, array, myriad) ; **lie** (1. be stretched out, sprawl, rest,
    repose, relax, lounge ; 2. be situated, be located… ; 3. be buried, be interred ; 4. remain,
    continue, stay, be ; 5. press down, weigh down… ; LIE IN : consist, be inherent, be present,
    exist, reside ; LIE LOW : hide, go into hiding…).
    *Clé (guide p.34)* : « host noun — *a host of people gathered for the march* : multitude, crowd,
    throng, mob, army, herd, pack, flock, swarm, troop, band, mass, assembly, array, myriad. — lie
    verb 1. *he was lying not sitting* : be stretched out, sprawl, rest, repose, relax, lounge. »
    (donc, dans le poème : *host* = multitude ; *lie* = être étendu.)
15. <p.177> **D. Summary** : « Complete the summary of the poem with the words in the box. » Boîte
    (10 mots) : `wandering ; dance ; impression ; actual ; day ; reawakened ; walking ; host ;
    energy ; alive`. Résumé à 10 trous.
    *Clé (guide p.34)* : « In Daffodils, Wordsworth is remembering a **day** when he was out
    **walking** and saw his **host** of daffodils. In this poem, the poet is **wandering** alone
    through the countryside. In his reminiscence of this **dance** of the daffodils, his ego is
    reinvested with **energy** and, it may be assumed, **reawakened** to a life more "**alive**" than
    the one of his **actual** reality. He is looking back on how much of an **impression** it has had
    on him. »
16. <p.178> **HOW FAMILIAR ARE YOU WITH THE FEATURES OF FIGURATIVE LANGUAGE?** — activité **1**
    (encadré verbatim) : « Figurative language is a form of language use in which writers and
    speakers convey something other than the literal meaning of their words. » Exemples imprimés :
    a) **Metaphor** — « A comparison between essentially unlike things without an explicitly
    comparative word such as *like* or *as*. An example is "My love is a red, red rose," from
    Burns's "A Red, Red Rose." » + citation d'Aristote (*Rhetoric*) à reformuler : « It is metaphor
    above all else that gives cleanness, charm and distinction to the style ». b) **Simile** —
    « A figure of speech involving a comparison between unlike things using *like*, *as*, or *as
    though*. An example : "My love is like a red, red rose." » c) **Hyperbole or exaggeration.**
    d) **Personification** : « endowing inanimate objects with human characteristics. »
17. <p.178> activité **2** : a) « find a simile in stanza 1. What characterizes the 'lonely' cloud?
    what does the comparison between the poet and the cloud suggest? Justify your answer. »
    b) « pick out another simile in stanza 2. » c) « Wordsworth uses personification in this poem.
    Pick out the details showing the use of this figure of speech. » d) « check if the poem contains
    a metaphor. » e) « go back to 2 a and b above and try to convert each simile into a metaphor. »
    *Clé (guide p.35)* : a) la comparaison d'ouverture entre le poète et un nuage solitaire →
    « lonely cloud → freedom, no constraints but also no real aim, hence the use of the verb
    'wander' » ; b) la comparaison des jonquilles aux étoiles au début de la strophe 2 ;
    c) « Wordsworth uses personification in this poem : i- The dances of the daffodils ; ii- Endowing
    the daffodils with verbs of motion and words (glee, tossing, fluttering, …) ».
18. <p.178> **E.** « Read the poem aloud in an expressive way. »
19. <p.178> **F. HOW GOOD AN ARTIST ARE YOU?** « Can you draw a picture of the landscape described in
    the poem? »

**Encadrés officiels (verbatim, p.179)**

> MY NEW WORDS
>
> daffodils, gaze, host, lie, wandering, twinkle, glance, sparkle, bliss.

> WHAT I CAN DO NOW
>
> • Read a poem and understand its aesthetic value
> • Identify figurative language
> • Understand entries in a dictionary page
> • Check the meaning of words in context
> • Summarise a poem
> • Draw a picture
> • Anything else?
> ……………….?……………
> ……………….?……………
> ……………….?……………

(pas de colonne `MY GRAMMAR` dans cette leçon)

**Lexique de la leçon** : daffodils, gaze, host, lie, wandering, twinkle, glance, sparkle, bliss ;
termes de poésie : stanza, alliteration, rhyme (internal rhyme / end rhyme), figurative language,
metaphor, simile, hyperbole, personification, tone ; *wander* vs *wonder* ; formes archaïques
*o'er* (= over), *oft* (= often) ; champ lexical du bonheur : fluttering, dancing, glee, gay,
pleasure, bliss.

#### Lesson 9 — WRITING AS A PROCESS (p.180–181)

- **Skills blend** : — (aucun bandeau de blend ; seul le sous-bandeau **IN GROUPS** est imprimé)
- **Constructing meaning** : —
- **Communicating** : Writing an argumentative article
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : —
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.180) : diagramme **STEPS IN WRITING** — 5 boîtes reliées par des empreintes de pas :
*Generating ideas* → *Planning and Organising* → *First Draft* → *Revising and Editing* →
*Final Draft*. (p.181) diagramme des traits du texte argumentatif : au centre *Argumentative Text*,
autour *Controversial ideas*, *Counter with tact*, *Problems (main thesis)*, *Win the reader to
one's side (find common ground)*, *Connectors*, *Supporting Arguments for or against*, et en tête
« Main thesis (presenting the problem) » avec une ligne à compléter `1. …………. ? ……………….`

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.180> **IN GROUPS** — activité **1** : « Do you remember the different steps in the writing
   process? Here they are in a scrambled order. Reorder them. » a) revise, rewrite as many times as
   necessary ; b) share and respond (peer review) ; c) generate ideas through brainstorming, mind
   mapping, using a diagram of ideas, questionnaires ; d) make final adjustments in format and
   layout ; e) organize points / ideas, follow a model outline (selecting and focusing information,
   combining information) ; f) write a first draft.
   *Clé (guide p.35)* : « Correct order of the steps : c – e – f – b – a – d. »
2. <p.180> activité **2** : « The following diagram summarises the various steps in the writing
   process. One step is missing. Identify it. » (diagramme STEPS IN WRITING).
   *Clé (guide p.35)* : « Missing step : peer review. »
3. <p.181> « The diagram below summarizes the features of argumentative texts. Study the diagram and
   find out the main components of an argumentative text. »
4. <p.181> **WRITE AN ARGUMENTATIVE ARTICLE** : « Your teacher asked you to write an argumentative
   article for your school magazine. The topic is the negative effects of technology on people's
   life in that it controls and overwhelms their lives. Refer to the diagram above and the steps of
   the process to produce your article. » Connecteurs imposés : *however, although, therefore, in
   contrast, furthermore, on the contrary, supposing that, looked at another way.*

**Encadrés officiels** : aucun (la leçon 9 ne comporte ni `MY NEW WORDS`, ni `MY GRAMMAR`, ni
`WHAT I CAN DO NOW`).

**Lexique de la leçon** : brainstorming, mind mapping, outline, first draft, final draft, peer
review, revising and editing, format and layout, proofread ; main thesis, supporting arguments,
counter-arguments, controversial ideas, common ground, connectors ; linkers *however, although,
therefore, in contrast, furthermore, on the contrary, supposing that, looked at another way*.

#### For Arts Students : Session 5 — Appreciating The BARD'S SONNET 18 and Some Quotations from his PLAYS (p.182–183)

- **Public** : ⚠️ Arts students uniquement (section Lettres — 5 h/semaine ; hors tronc commun)
- **Skills blend** : READING and PARAPHRASING (bandeau p.182)
- **Constructing meaning** : Reading a sonnet
- **Communicating** : Speaking — Paraphrasing quotations and a stanza from a poem
- **Functions & linguistic exponents** : Punctuation Marks (Recognition and Use)
- **Pronunciation** : —

**Supports** (p.182) : le **Sonnet 18 de William Shakespeare**, imprimé sur deux colonnes —
**SONNET 18** (texte original, 14 vers en 3 quatrains + 1 distique) en regard de **PARAPHRASE**
(reformulation en anglais moderne, ligne à ligne). ⚠️ **Aucun vers n'est reproduit ici** (droits +
R-2) : le poète compare l'être aimé à un jour d'été, montre que l'été est bref et que toute beauté
se fane, puis affirme que la beauté de l'aimé ne se fanera pas parce que le poème lui-même
l'immortalise. (p.183) tableau des rimes à compléter.

**Déroulé des activités**

1. <p.182> **READING and PARAPHRASING — A.** « Read Shakespeare's sonnet and its paraphrase. »
   activité **1** : « Check what it is about. » a) The profound joy and beauty of love b) The
   description of nature in comparison with love c) The power of love to immortalize poetry and the
   subject of that poetry.
   *Clé (guide p.35)* : **1-c**.
2. <p.182> activité **2** : « The purpose of this sonnet is to a) express the poet's profound
   feelings b) describe summer and its beauty c) make sure his beloved knows the role of poetry. »
   *Clé (guide p.35)* : **2-a**.
3. <p.183> activité **3** : « Read the Sonnet and its paraphrase and identify the differences.
   Example : thee → you ; Thou → you » (repérage du lexique du XVI<sup>e</sup> siècle : *thee, thou,
   thy, art, hath, owest, growest, dimm'd, untrimm'd, wander'st*).
4. <p.183> **B. Focus on Rhymes** — activité **1** : « How many lines has the sonnet got? »
   (réponse : 14).
5. <p.183> activité **2** : tableau donné pour les 4 premiers vers — Lines 1/2/3/4 → Rhyme a/b/a/b.
6. <p.183> activité **3** : « Now do the same with lines 5 to 8 and lines 9 to 12. Identify the
   rhymes. » Tableau à compléter : 5 → c (donné), 6 → d (donné), 7 → ------, 8 → ------, 9 → e
   (donné), 10 → f (donné), 11 → ------, 12 → ------, 13 → g (donné), 14 → ------.
   (attendu : 7 → c, 8 → d, 11 → e, 12 → f, 14 → g, soit *abab cdcd efef gg*.)
7. <p.183> activité **4** : « Can you define a sonnet, now that you know its components and features?
   Complete the following definition. A sonnet is a poem composed of ------ quatrains and -------
   couplet. A quatrain is a stanza of ------ lines. A couplet consists of ------ lines. »
   *Clé (guide p.35)* : « A sonnet is a poem composed of **3** quatrains and **1** couplet.
   A quatrain is a stanza of **4** lines. A couplet consists of **2** lines. »
8. <p.183> activité **5** : « Circle the better word that fits each sentence. » Texte à choix
   binaires : « The three quatrains of this sonnet (are - are not) of a (parallel - different)
   structure. The poet tries to describe his beloved who is compared to summer in its beauty. In the
   (first - final) couplet, the poet reaffirms his hope that as (long - far) as mankind exists, his
   poetry will live on, (thus - however) ensuring the immortality of his muse. »
   *Clé (guide p.35)* : are – parallel – final – long – thus.
9. <p.183> **C. How good are you at paraphrasing?** « Choose a stanza from Wordsworth's poem *The
   Daffodils* and paraphrase it. » (renvoi explicite à la leçon 8).
